# Codex Kernel Audit Progress

## 2026-10-17 — White-screen report and kernel image boundary

- **Owner report:** the `files/candidate/7.4.6/ezkernelnew.bin` build also boots to a white screen. The hardware revision used for this specific attempt was not stated; treat it as direct failure evidence for this artifact, not proof of a board-specific cause.
- **Controlled build comparison:** using the same devkitARM 16.1.0 toolchain, v7.4.5b commit `0f5fe5a` builds to 2,096,272 bytes (880 bytes below 2 MiB), matching the published v7.4.5b asset size. The source at `e2a8d97` builds to 2,094,120 bytes. The first measured post-release image over the documented 2-MiB kernel allocation is commit `2726d13` at 2,097,192 bytes (40 bytes over); the v7.4.6-test snapshot `c2a2faf` is 2,097,256 bytes (104 over); the current candidate is 2,097,280 bytes (128 over). Thus the overflow is already present in the test snapshot, not caused only by the later NOR-format audit fix.
- **Load-address evidence:** the official board allocation table previously reviewed for F-032 places the kernel in a 16-bit-address range `0x020000` to `<0x120000`, i.e. 0x100000 x16 words = 2 MiB. In the v7.4.6-test ELF, `.init_array` is linked at VMA `0x03007C20` with LMA `0x08200058`; `.fini_array` follows at `0x0820005C`. The initialized-data source begins at `0x081FE29C` and the startup copy range ends at ROM LMA `0x08200060`, crossing the `0x08200000` 2-MiB boundary. The current candidate places these arrays later still (`.init_array` LMA `0x08200070`, `.fini_array` LMA `0x08200074`, data-copy end `0x08200078`). Startup uses the linked data range and invokes `__libc_init_array`, so if the updater limits the kernel slot to its documented 2 MiB, required startup data can be missing. This is a strong, hardware-agnostic explanation for a white screen, but the external bootloader's exact write/read limit is not in this source tree; do not call causality proven until that behavior is verified.
- **Artifact format check:** the requested candidate is a raw `.gba` image copied to the `.bin` filename, matching this repository's `build.bat` and the historical raw `files/ezkernelnew.bin` (2,089,664 bytes) that the owner reported booting. The upstream vendor's stock tool separately combines `.gba` with `image.bin` into an update package; that combined package size is not comparable to the raw kernel slot. Do not replace the candidate with a differently packaged image without establishing which updater format the user's hardware test used.
- **Regression boundary:** the first over-limit build is the large source checkpoint `2726d13`, which introduces many post-v7.4.5b changes at once; this does not isolate one code function as the cause. Later `0e81fee` save/cheat changes account for part of the additional growth by the test snapshot. Preserve all v7.4.5b behavior and do not apply a board-specific workaround. The current artifact remains unchanged pending a size-preserving solution and confirmation of the updater's limit.
- **Disposition/next:** prioritize verifying the 2-MiB kernel-slot enforcement and finding a surgical, revision-agnostic way to keep the complete raw kernel image within that allocation while retaining the v7.4.5b release and independently justified post-release changes. Add a build-time size guard once the raw-image limit is confirmed. Keep the settings-loss and Rev. B browsing-slowdown reports separate; neither is explained by image size alone. No hardware flash was performed from this environment.

## 2026-10-17 — Compare release and candidate working-memory layouts

- **Owner hypothesis:** original Omega DE may run out of working memory on v7.4.6-test while DE Rev. B has more headroom. Investigate this without assuming the revisions have different RAM or introducing revision-specific code.
- **Controlled link comparison:** built v7.4.5b source commit `0f5fe5a` in an isolated worktree with the same devkitARM 16.1.0 toolchain used for the corrected candidate. Both images link `__eheap_start` / `__sbss_end__` at `0x02037938`, `__eheap_end` at `0x0203C000`, and `__sp_usr` at `0x0203F000`. The linker-defined EWRAM heap interval is therefore 18,120 bytes in both builds; the 12,288-byte gap from heap end to user-stack top is also unchanged. These are configured bounds, not runtime free-space or high-water measurements.
- **Static memory delta:** `.sbss` is exactly `0x37938` bytes in both builds. Candidate `.data` is unchanged at `0x1DBC`; `.iwram` is 192 bytes smaller and IWRAM `.bss` is 8 bytes smaller than v7.4.5b. No evidence of additional static EWRAM/IWRAM consumption in the post-release candidate, or of a static-memory exhaustion mechanism.
- **Runtime-path check:** the post-release diff adds no heap-allocation call in the changed firmware paths. The normal SD title-list/scroll implementation is unchanged; the only menu-scroll hunk in `ezkernelnew.c` is for the NOR menu. Timer1 audio stop/IRQ functions were moved into IWRAM, and other changed paths concern NOR operations, settings/save error reporting, first-time save creation, and cheat parsing. None establishes a new EWRAM exhaustion path during ordinary SD browsing.
- **Hardware limits:** available product documentation records 32 MiB cartridge PSRAM for the original Omega DE; the previously documented DE B distinction is NOR capacity, not evidence of extra working RAM. The GBA's internal EWRAM is console memory, not a cartridge-revision resource. No verified specification in this audit establishes extra working RAM on Rev. B.
- **Disposition:** the simple theory that v7.4.6-test statically used up more working RAM is not supported. Runtime stack depth, peak heap use, allocator fragmentation, and exact Rev. B memory/timing differences remain unmeasured, so a runtime exhaustion hypothesis is not disproved. Keep it open pending matched hardware measurements or defensible runtime instrumentation; do not make a board-specific change.
- **Corrected candidate:** restored the v7.4.5b linker stack/heap values and both `__sp_usr` reset operands; preserved the F-027 reset trampoline, LFN placements, and later changes. Target build and audit script pass. The updated `files/candidate/ezkernelnew-stack-relocation.bin` is 2,097,280 bytes, SHA-256 `2d98d0ad6b714930bb90cd1f86943b0e17dc576bd517597f97d47e9fa12e5efa`, byte-identical to the Makefile `.gba`. This is a test candidate, not hardware validation or a confirmed symptom fix.

## 2026-10-17 18:42 UTC — NOR format leaves persistent catalog stale

- **Phase/subsystem:** S98 NOR format and S71 catalog persistence. **Objective:** establish whether a successful format keeps the persistent catalog coherent with erased game data, and whether the correction can use existing cross-revision-safe APIs.
- **Verified source path:** `FormatNor()` calls `Chip_Erase()`, then zeroes the in-memory `pNorFS`, and returns success without calling `Save_NOR_info()`. The UI caller only reports `FormatNor()==2` as a failure. The metadata catalog is read from S71 at startup and on NOR-list refresh (`Read_NOR_info()`), while `GetFileListFromNor()` scans S98 and uses catalog entries to validate contiguous game records. Main startup has a special zero-game cleanup that later saves a cleared catalog; list refresh does not do that cleanup. Thus a successful format leaves stale persistent metadata available to be reloaded during the same session, even though the S98 payload was erased.
- **Impact/disposition:** verified low-severity metadata consistency defect in the format-success path; it does not change the physical erase result, and the scanner avoids counting erased ROM contents. A narrow correction is available: clear the RAM catalog and persist it through the existing `Save_NOR_info()` callback immediately after a successful chip erase. This uses the already-established S71 metadata write path and does not depend on board revision, flash ID changes, new offsets, or a new recovery format. If metadata persistence fails, report it; the physical format has already completed, so the empty RAM catalog must not be rolled back to stale entries.
- **Planned regression coverage:** extend the existing host-testable catalog helper with a clear-and-persist operation, asserting that the callback sees an all-zero catalog, that persistence failure is surfaced, and that the empty runtime catalog remains empty after failure. Integrate it into `FormatNor()` and route the new status to the existing save-error notification. Test on host; physical NOR behavior remains unverified.
- **Scope update:** the primary F-022 caller review found no broad missing error propagation for erase/program operations; clean/program failures flow through `Loadfile2NOR()` to the existing UI error path. Continue this finding as a targeted NOR metadata-consistency fix rather than changing flash status protocols or timings.

## 2026-10-17 18:29 UTC — F-036 staged-replacement design review

- **Phase/subsystem:** SD save replacement and FatFs interruption recovery. **Objective:** assess whether the existing-save loss has a small, testable correction compatible with the owner's hardware-revision-agnostic rule.
- **Investigation:** re-read `Save_savefile`, `SavefileWrite`, `Process_savefile`, `Check_save_flag`, and `FRAM_save_op` in `source/ezkernelnew.c`; inspected repository FatFs `f_open`, `f_sync`, `f_unlink`, and `f_rename` in `source/ff15/ff.c`; inspected LFN/FatFs configuration and `Backup_savefile`; ran the focused save host suite.
- **Verified findings:** `FA_CREATE_ALWAYS` clears the existing FAT directory entry's cluster and size and removes the old chain before the replacement bytes have been written. `f_rename` checks for an absent destination, registers its new directory entry and only then removes the old entry; FatFs labels this as an interruption-sensitive critical section. `f_unlink` removes the directory entry before freeing its cluster chain. Therefore neither a simple temp-file rename nor backup-plus-rename is an atomic replacement. `Backup_savefile()` rotates files while ignoring unlink/rename/copy results and has no restoration call; it does not close F-036.
- **Caller/recovery scope:** failed startup export in `Check_save_flag()` leaves the flash recovery marker set and reports an error; manual `FRAM_save_op(2)` reports error 5. Ordinary `Process_savefile()` loads the current target and may optionally create a backup, but no backup recovery exists. A staged design would need deterministic recovery before all affected loads and writes, full-LFN-safe transaction identity, and idempotency across failures in the journal/recovery operations themselves.
- **Validation:** `bash scripts/test-savefile-host.sh` PASS (20 checks) and `git diff --check` PASS. The host scenario confirms the failed long-name overwrite returns failure and leaves `f_stat` reporting `FR_NO_FILE`. This test models the repository FatFs implementation and injected disk-write failures; it does not establish physical SD-card power-cut or torn-sector guarantees.
- **Disposition:** F-036 remains a verified Medium risk and is not fixed. No production change is justified by a simple rename/unlink design. A more complete redundant journal/recovery design would have broad file-path and compatibility scope; its durability assumptions and crash-cut state machine have not been established. This finding and its proposed treatment are generic to FatFs save handling and do not require board-revision-specific behavior.
- **Next:** resume F-022's accepted-device/status protocol and complete timing-bound review. Do not add an arbitrary timeout or hardware-specific branch. Any eventual correction must be valid for all accepted devices and board revisions; if complete bounds are unavailable, preserve the no-timeout disposition.

## 2026-10-17 18:29 UTC — F-022 current-source caller and device-bound reconciliation

- **Phase/subsystem:** NOR flash status polling, ID dispatch, and result propagation. **Objective:** verify the current branch after the prior F-022 notes and decide whether a bounded-wait or device-dispatch change is supportable under the hardware-revision-agnostic constraint.
- **Current source trace:** `Loadfile2NOR()` accepts only S98 autoselect X0E `0x223D`; it checks `Block_Erase()` and `WriteFlash_with32word()` results and commits the catalog only after data programming and file close. `NOR_EraseAllPPB()` has a separate unbounded DQ6/DQ5 poll followed by DQ0 verification. `Save_info()` reads S71 X0E; `0x2202` takes the word-program path, while every other ID takes the GL064-style buffer path. This remains an unverified acceptance policy because no exact alternate S71 ID set or physical board BOM/ID capture is available.
- **Current source result-path correction:** this checkout has `u8`-returning `Save_info`/`Save_sav_info`/`Save_NOR_info`/`Save_SET_info`, not the older `void` API described in the 2026-10-15 21:53 historical entry. Direct callers check results or report errors; `NOR_PersistCatalog()` forwards the metadata-save result into the catalog transaction. Startup save recovery retains its marker when save output fails. Keep the older note as historical; it is not the current caller contract.
- **Status/timing evidence:** `Wait_Flash_Operation()` classifies DQ6 toggling, confirms DQ5, checks DQ1 during write-buffer abort, and then requires stable full-word readback. Neither this loop nor `NOR_EraseAllPPB()` has a finite wall-clock bound. The cited Infineon WS512P (`0x223D`) timing tables give finite nominal maxima, but the reviewed sector erase maximum explicitly excludes the embedded zero-preprogram phase. The PL064J (`0x2202`) spec has a similar exclusion; the generic alternate S71 branch has no established exact part. Previously recorded official references and table details are in the 2026-10-16 23:40 and 2026-10-15 22:13 entries.
- **Validation:** `bash scripts/test-nor-flash-status-host.sh`, `bash scripts/test-nor-catalog-transaction-host.sh`, and `bash scripts/test-nor-catalog-recovery-host.sh` PASS. These test pure classification/transaction logic, not chip status waveforms, device timing, or physical NOR.
- **Disposition:** no timeout, ID whitelist, or board-specific branch is justified. A new timeout would lack a complete bound for every accepted device and embedded operation; tightening the S71 ID set without identifying all supported chips risks rejecting working hardware and cannot be validated against a board matrix. The code remains unchanged. F-022 stays Medium/open for liveness and unsupported-ID uncertainty; this is not evidence that normal boards hang.
- **Next:** proceed to F-032's NOR catalog interruption-recovery design only as a source/host investigation. Do not reserve or use an undocumented flash sector, add a revision branch, or integrate a recovery format without proof that the same storage contract exists across supported revisions. If no common, reserved storage is established, retain F-032 without a production fix.

## 2026-10-17 18:29 UTC — F-032 catalog recovery design constrained by storage evidence

- **Phase/subsystem:** auxiliary NOR catalog persistence and interrupted updates. **Objective:** determine whether the current evidence supports any generic recovery change without selecting hardware-revision-specific storage.
- **Investigation:** read `source/ez_define.h`, `source/Ezcard_OP.c::Save_info/Save_NOR_info/Read_NOR_info`, `source/NORflash_OP.c::NOR_PersistCatalog/Loadfile2NOR`, and the production `nor_catalog_transaction.h`; reviewed the characterization-only `nor_catalog_recovery.h` and `tests/nor-catalog-recovery-host.c`.
- **Verified behavior:** the catalog is stored at `NOR_info_offset=0x7A0000`; each metadata save erases a full 64-KiB S71 sector before rewriting the 8,448-byte `pNorFS` payload. `NOR_CommitCatalogSlot()` restores the in-memory catalog on a returned persistence error but cannot restore persistent data after power loss. `nor_catalog_recovery.h` is not included in production code. Its host model already demonstrates that an unretired stale shadow can be selected after legacy firmware erases/replaces the primary.
- **Storage/compatibility limitation:** `0x7C0000` and any second copy location are not assigned by the current source; there is no cross-revision BOM, S71 capacity/geometry proof, or allocation contract showing such storage is reserved on every supported board. The prior secondary-header design can also conflict with older firmware that rewrites the primary without updating the shadow. No common, legacy-compatible recovery protocol has been established.

## 2026-10-17 18:29 UTC — F-011 reflash/settings paths traced

- **Phase/subsystem:** kernel-update staging and settings persistence. **Objective:** determine whether repository code connects the reported settings loss after re-flashing to either SD `SETTINGS.TXT` or the in-cart S71 settings area, without pursuing a revision-specific fix.

In-cart settings reside at `SET_info_offset=0x7B0000` and are rewritten only through explicit settings/menu save paths and `Set_saveMODE()`, using `Save_info()` to erase/reprogram the whole 64-KiB sector. The traced normal startup path reads settings but does not invoke `Save_SET_info()`.
- **Distinct generic persistence risk:** `/SYSTEM/SETTINGS.TXT` remains a separate SD file. Its `Launcher_SaveUnifiedSettings()` uses `FA_CREATE_ALWAYS` and ignores print/close status, so a post-open write failure can truncate the existing document. The prior F-011 analysis judged a journal/redundant transaction disproportionate and contrary to the owner's known-defaults preference; checking errors alone cannot restore the previous contents.
- **Unverified owner report:** the repository does not contain the external official firmware updater's actual write/erase routine, so source cannot establish whether re-flashing touches `0x7B0000`, nor why the reported working reflash loses settings. Do not attribute this to the SD file, S71 erase, or a board revision without a reproducible trace.
- **Disposition:** no production change. No small safe source fix links the current staging path to in-cart settings loss; a recovery protocol for the S71 sector would need a proven common storage contract, which remains absent. No revision-specific fix is pursued.

No hardware, kernel flash, or settings write was performed.

## 2026-10-17 00:33 UTC — F-022 progress callback and interrupt-state review

- **Phase/subsystem:** F-022 NOR liveness and IRQ safety. **Objective:** resolve the older open question about `VBlankIntrWait()` inside chip-erase progress callbacks while `Chip_Erase()` has disabled master interrupts.
- **Investigation:** inspected `source/NORflash_OP.c::Chip_Erase` and `NOR_ChipEraseProgress`, `source/Ezcard_OP.c::Wait_Flash_Operation` and `Save_info`, `source/nor_flash_status.h`, and all `Wait_Flash_Operation` callers. `Chip_Erase()` saves `REG_IME`, clears it before S98 chip erase, and restores the saved value on each exit. Its status-poll callback runs every 65,536 busy samples; `Wait_Flash_Operation()` saves the caller's IME before the callback and restores that value after it. The callback itself invokes `VBlankIntrWait()` three times.
- **BIOS evidence:** devkitPro's `gba_systemcalls.h` maps `VBlankIntrWait()` to BIOS SWI 5. The GBATEK GBA-only reference describes SWI 5 as IntrWait with VBlank and says IntrWait forcefully sets IME=1; the gba Rust BIOS API independently documents that IntrWait forces IME on and may run the interrupt handler before returning. Sources: [devkitPro libgba](https://github.com/devkitPro/libgba/blob/master/include/gba_systemcalls.h), [GBATEK SWI reference](https://rust-console.github.io/gbatek-gbaonly/), [gba BIOS IntrWait documentation](https://docs.rs/gba/latest/gba/bios/fn.IntrWait.html). In the firmware's active UI path, `main()` calls `irqInit()`, enables VBlank, then sets `REG_IME=1` (`source/ezkernelnew.c:14652-14654`).
- **Verified behavior / disposition:** a progress callback reached from the polling loop can temporarily cause IME to become 1 inside BIOS `VBlankIntrWait`, but `Wait_Flash_Operation()` restores the callback-entry value (0) before resuming the polling loop. This explains the apparent IME transition and confirms the callback wrapper's purpose; no new defect or safe source change was established. `NOR_ChipEraseProgress()` is also called directly during post-erase readback, outside the poll wrapper; BIOS waiting can enable IRQs during that scan, after which `Chip_Erase()` restores its originally saved IME before return. The current call path enters from `FormatNor()` in the UI, where VBlank is enabled. No physical flash operation was performed.
- **Still open:** `Wait_Flash_Operation()` has no finite wall-clock limit while status remains busy/toggling indefinitely. The previously reviewed device timing tables exclude an internal erase preprogram phase, and supported alternate S71 IDs remain undocumented, so no universal timeout is justified. The per-device status protocol and complete accepted-ID set also remain open; retain F-022 Medium.
- **Commands/validation:** searched all wait/erase callers and IRQ setup; read the local C call paths and classifier; browsed the cited BIOS API references. No build, host test, code change, or hardware flash operation was run.
- **Next:** keep the current no-arbitrary-timeout disposition; seek primary evidence for every supported S71/S98 part and complete operation timing before designing a bounded wait. Then review remaining F-022 caller/reporting paths and F-032/F-036 recovery designs. The old progress note at 2026-10-15 23:xx requesting the SWI/IME check is resolved by this entry.
- **Git status:** local `main` remains at `c2a2faf39ce10d7ca5e1c30ce3ec55917d1dd743`, one commit ahead of `origin/main`; audit ledgers are uncommitted, and `files/` remains user-owned untracked content.

## 2026-10-16 23:45 UTC — F-022 S71 interrupt hazard confirmed; narrow fix planned

- **Phase/subsystem:** S71 NOR metadata operations and interrupt dispatch. **Objective:** verify whether IRQ dispatch can fetch instructions from the S71 flash while `Save_info()` runs S71 ID/erase/program commands, and identify a narrow safe response.
- **Evidence:** `main()` calls `irqInit()`, enables `IRQ_VBLANK`, and turns on `REG_IME`. A VBlank during embedded S71 busy mode can therefore dispatch into the same flash device being operated on. This is a **likely High impact defect** (possible invalid instruction fetch/system stall during save/catalog/settings metadata operations), source- and linked-address-supported, but not physically reproduced.
- **Timer1 path:** `UIAudio_Timer1IRQ`, `UIAudio_StopFromTimer`, and `UIAudio_StopHardware` are explicitly `IWRAM_CODE`; this handler performs only IWRAM code and direct audio/timer/DMA register writes. `UIAudio_Init()` registers/enables Timer1, and main separately enables VBlank. The narrow approach is to mask only VBlank in `REG_IE` across the full `Save_info()` interval, preserving/restoring the original `REG_IE` on every success/error exit; leave Timer1 enabled. Do not globally clear `REG_IME` or alter S98 routines.

## 2026-10-16 23:40 UTC — NOR timing limits and mapped-window model checked

- **Phase/subsystem:** F-022 operation liveness; F-037 S98 device topology. **Objective:** determine what official timing data can bound and what `SetRompage` proves about the 64-MiB NOR address model.
- **Source investigation:** inspected `Wait_Flash_Operation` (`source/Ezcard_OP.c`), `SetRompage`, `Block_Erase`, `Chip_Erase`, `WriteFlash_with32word`, `NOR_EraseAllPPB`, and `Loadfile2NOR` (`source/NORflash_OP.c`); checked `gl_currentpage` initialization and every window/page increment. `NOR_CAPACITY_BYTES=0x04000000`, `NOR_WINDOW_BYTES=0x00800000`, and each 8-MiB crossing increments the C4 page value by `0x1000`; `SetRompage` writes that value to `0x09880000`. Chip erase verifies eight windows. This verifies the firmware's logical mapping contract, not physical die/device count or board wiring.
- **Primary timing evidence:** Infineon's WS512P datasheet table lists 64-Kword (128-KiB) sector erase max 3.0 s and 16-Kword (32-KiB) erase max 1.75 s, but explicitly says these exclude the embedded `00h` programming phase. The same datasheet says the embedded erase algorithm programs/verifies the memory before electrical erase. It lists WS512P chip erase max 616 s; the table does not state that this row excludes preprogram, so do not infer either way. Single-word max is 400 us (excluding command overhead); total 32-word buffered-program max is 3000 us. Infineon GL-S datasheet lists model-family 64-KiB sector erase max 1000 ms including `00h` preprogram, and 65.4 s chip erase max; this is useful only if GL-S topology/fitment is proven.
- **PPB timing check:** WS512P PPB procedure requires waiting 500 us before the first status reads; `NOR_EraseAllPPB` waits with `delay(3000)`, then polls DQ6/DQ5 and verifies DQ0, and exits PPB mode on either result. The source `delay` is a volatile decrement loop in GamePak text, not a calibrated timer. Its exact minimum duration under runtime bus wait states has not been established; keep this timing point unverified rather than assuming 3000 units means microseconds.
- **F-022 disposition:** a universal timeout remains unjustified. The accepted WS512P sector-erase maximum excludes an internal phase with no published bound in the reviewed table, and S71's generic path includes IDs whose supported models/maxima are unknown. `Wait_Flash_Operation` and the separate PPB loop remain infinite if status keeps showing busy forever. No production change was made; device-specific timeout work needs exact supported OPNs and a calibrated monotonic timer/timeout policy, plus timeout/partial-operation behavior tests.
- **F-037 topology refinement:** the source's 64-MiB/eight-window model is consistent with one 512-Mbit WS512P logical address space, and the 128-KiB block plus four 32-KiB endpoint erases matches that model's documented main/boot geometry. A candidate `S29GL064S...020` is 64 Mbit/8 MiB with 64-KiB sectors, so the source path's accepted 64-MiB geometry would require additional board/page-selection facts (for example, whether pages select separate devices or portions of a larger array). No official BOM/physical ID has surfaced. Keep F-037 conditional and do not change ID or erase behavior.
- **Commands/results:** read-only source/ELF inspection and official Infineon datasheet review only; no code test was necessary because source was unchanged. Previous NOR host tests remain the validation baseline: both status and catalog-recovery scripts passed at 23:36 UTC. No hardware NOR access.
- **Next:** determine whether official EZ-FLASH board/product documents or source identify the S98 `020` OPN/topology; if unavailable, record exact evidence gap. Model 02 uses uniform 64-KB sectors; model 04 is the bottom-boot variant with smaller 8-KB sectors at the bottom. This matches the code's 16-bit x16 command addressing: `Read_S98NOR_ID()` reads X0E via `FlashBase_S98 + 0xE*2`, but `Loadfile2NOR()` accepts only `0x223D`. Conditional finding: if this documented `...020` GL-S part is the S98 fitted to a supported device, this firmware rejects NOR installation before opening the ROM. Official EZ-FLASH Firmware 7 notes say Rev.B support was added for S98 chips with `020` suffixes, but no BOM or physical cartridge ID is available to prove that this exact Infineon part is fitted. No ID allowlist change is justified without confirmation and a full erase-geometry review.
- **Potential geometry hazard:** `NOR_BLOCK_SIZE` is 0x20000 (128 KiB). For non-edge blocks, `Block_Erase()` issues a single sector erase at `Address` but verifies 0x20000 bytes; the documented GL-S model 02 sectors are 0x10000 bytes each. If the code's block address maps byte-for-byte to this device and the second sector contains non-FF data, the one-sector command cannot erase the full logical block. The special first/last-block path issues commands every 0x8000 bytes and suggests the legacy device may use different geometry. Treat the GL-S erase interaction as a high-risk incompatibility hypothesis until board/mapper semantics and all supported S98 models are established; do not enable this part based only on the ID match.

Checked `NOR_BLOCK_SIZE=0x20000`, `NOR_WINDOW_BYTES=0x800000`, 64-MiB capacity, and page changes in 8-MiB increments. `Block_Erase` maps a logical byte offset to a page, issues one sector-erase command for ordinary blocks, then verifies 128 KiB. Its first/last-block path issues four commands at 32-KiB steps.
- **Verified source/device facts:** the code reads the x16 autoselect ID cycle at word X0E (`FlashBase_S98 + 0xE*2`) and only accepts `0x223D`. Infineon's official S29WS512P datasheet identifies X0E `0x223D` as WS512P; it documents 128-KiB main sectors and 32-KiB top/bottom boot sectors, matching this code's 128-KiB blocks and endpoint splitting. The official Infineon S29GL064S product `S29GL064S80FHV020` is a GL-S 64-Mbit/8-MiB part. Its x16 datasheet gives the X0E ID word `0x220C` for model 02 and 64-KiB sectors throughout model 02. EZ-FLASH's official Firmware 7 changelog separately says Rev.B support was added for S98 chips with `020` suffixes.
- **Likely finding F-037, High, conditional on fitment:** if `S29GL064S80FHV020` is among the S98 parts referred to by the official `020` support note, this kernel's NOR install is rejected before `f_open`, because `0x220C != 0x223D`. If ID rejection is bypassed, ordinary 128-KiB block erasure still sends only one sector command; on the documented 64-KiB layout, data in the second sector remains. The NOR delete path also calls this helper on an occupied last-title range, so it could erase half a block and then fail whole-block verification. Expected behavior is revision-aware ID/geometry support with erase commands covering every physical sector; actual source supports only the WS512P-style ID/geometry. This is source-reproducible conditional behavior, not a physical cartridge reproduction.
- **Classification limit:** the official changelog proves that a `020` S98 suffix family exists and is intended for support, and Infineon proves the candidate part's ID/geometry. No board BOM, exact chip marking, or physical ID capture proves that this exact Infineon OPN is fitted on every Rev.B (or other) device. Treat the relationship as likely, not universal or hardware-verified. Primary references: [WS512P datasheet](https://www.infineon.com/assets/row/public/documents/10/49/infineon-s29ws512p-s29ws256p-s29ws128p-512-256-128-m-x-16-bit-1.8-v-simultaneous-read-write-flash-datasheet-en.pdf), [GL-S datasheet](https://www.infineon.com/assets/row/public/documents/10/49/infineon-s29gl064s-64-mbit8-mbyte3-datasheet-en.pdf), [S29GL064S80FHV020 product page](https://www.infineon.com/part/S29GL064S80FHV020), [EZ-FLASH changelog](https://www.ezflash.cn/download/).
- **Other F-022 caller mapping:** active S71 metadata erase, PL064 per-word program, alternate buffered program, and S98 block erase, chip erase, word program, and 16-word/32-byte buffer program use `Wait_Flash_Operation`. `WriteFlash` has no in-repository caller. S71 dispatch reads X0E; only `0x2202` selects PL064 word program and every other ID selects the GL-style buffer path. `Loadfile2NOR` checks block/program failures before catalog commit; `FormatNor` propagates `Chip_Erase` failure and verifies all 64 MiB afterward. Busy polling still has no finite timeout. A common limit is unjustified: accepted WS512P, PL064, and the undocumented alternate S71 path have different documented maxima, and some erase maxima exclude internal preprogram time.
- **History and validation:** `git log -S'0x223D' -- source/NORflash_OP.c` finds only the initial import `fb9d871` and no rationale or later revision-support change. `sh scripts/test-nor-flash-status-host.sh` PASS; `sh scripts/test-nor-catalog-recovery-host.sh` PASS; `git diff --check` PASS. These host tests do not emulate flash address decode or physical programming. No production code changed and no hardware flash operation occurred.
- **Next:** seek exact S98 part identification from an official BOM, board marking/photo, or user hardware capture. Before supporting `0x220C`, model whether one or multiple 8-MiB devices are selected by `SetRompage`; confirm capacity, sector mapping, and PPB behavior; then add a host command/geometry model and validate a narrow correction on sacrificial hardware. Preserve the current `0x223D` WS512P path. Continue F-022 timeout/recovery review and keep S71 alternate IDs open.

## 2026-10-16 23:13 UTC — F-011 settings writer rechecked against owner policy

- **Verified source behavior:** `Launcher_SaveUnifiedSettings()` at `source/ezkernelnew.c:11541` creates `/SYSTEM`, opens `/SYSTEM/SETTINGS.TXT` with `FA_WRITE | FA_CREATE_ALWAYS`, emits the whole file with many unchecked `f_printf()` calls, ignores `f_close()` status, then unlinks five legacy settings files and clears `launcher_settings_migration_pending` whenever open succeeded. This is separate from the unexplained custom-thumbnail startup stall.

## 2026-10-16 23:18 UTC — F-036 FatFs replacement semantics traced

- **Phase/subsystem:** FatFs save replacement and recovery. **Objective:** determine whether a simple staged or rename-based change can preserve an existing save safely after a failed write or reset.
- **Verified source evidence:** `source/ff15/ff.c::f_open` handles `FA_CREATE_ALWAYS` by zeroing the existing FAT file's cluster and size in its directory entry and freeing its old cluster chain; the exFAT path resets the file metadata and then removes its old chain. This occurs during open, before `Save_savefile` writes new SRAM bytes. `f_close` calls `f_sync`, which writes dirty file data and the updated directory entry and syncs the volume. On sync error, `f_close` does not reach the normal file-object invalidation. The failure path in `Save_savefile` ignores `f_unlink`'s result.
- **Rename/removal semantics:** `f_rename` checks that the destination does not already exist (`FR_EXIST`), registers the destination entry, then removes the source entry; the implementation labels the sequence interruption-sensitive. It is not an atomic overwrite primitive. `f_unlink` removes the directory entry, frees the cluster chain, and syncs the volume, so it also has multiple failure points. A naive temp-file plus two renames cannot be assumed crash-safe; an interrupted rename may leave duplicate names for one chain or an incomplete transaction.
- **Recovery call sites:** `Process_savefile()` changes to `/SYSTEM/SAVER` before opening/loading the save; startup `Check_save_flag()` changes there before exporting recovery data; manual `FRAM_save_op()` also changes to that directory before load/save. A staged replacement would need one recovery policy invoked before all three paths can read or replace a save. It also needs a collision-safe sidecar policy for full-length LFNs. No production change or new test was made; current focused regression evidence remains as in the 23:15 entry.
- **Conclusion:** F-036 remains a verified Medium risk and is not fixed. Existing FatFs operations provide no single-call atomic replace, and the required recovery protocol must handle interrupted directory updates as well as write failures. This is a design/validation gap, not an environment blocker; do not claim a backup/rename sketch is safe without a restart-after-each-write host model and tests for the actual FatFs implementation. Physical SD power-loss behavior remains untested.
- **Next:** specify a full-LFN sidecar naming policy and transaction state machine; add host restart/remount cut tests around write, sync, both rename steps, and cleanup; prove the final save path always resolves to a complete old or new file before integrating recovery into `Process_savefile`, `Check_save_flag`, and `FRAM_save_op`. If a safe policy cannot be proven, retain F-036 and resume F-022 liveness/device-ID evidence. Preserve user files and build artifacts; no commit, push, release modification, or hardware flash.

## 2026-10-16 22:46 UTC — F-032 caller and stale-shadow counterexample verified

- **Phase/subsystems:** S71 metadata recovery and S98 catalog matching. **Objective:** verify exact caller contracts and determine whether the integrated source/host model handles an interrupted legacy raw-catalog rewrite when the active shadow is already one generation old.
- **Caller/size evidence:** `rg` found only two direct `Save_NOR_info` call paths: `NOR_PersistCatalog()` during `Loadfile2NOR()` (passes `sizeof(FM_NOR_FS) * MAX_NOR`) and startup's zero-catalog normalization at `source/ezkernelnew.c:14774` (same full size). `sizeof(FM_NOR_FS)` is 132 bytes (source fields total 132 on target); 64 entries are 8,448 bytes, and the recovery image is 8,512 bytes, divisible by 32. `Read_NOR_info()` runs at startup and NOR-page re-entry. Both current `GetFileListFromNor()` callers reset `gl_norOffset` to zero first.
- **Scanner side-effect comparison:** the diff preserves the old scan checks and order: S98 header state at offsets `0xBE`, `0x06`, and `0xB2`; bounded block-aligned sizes; 16-byte title equality; bank advancement; save/restore of `REG_IME`; and restoration of the mapped `gl_currentpage`. The new local offset starts at zero and is copied to `gl_norOffset` only for the ordinary list scan. `NOR_CountCatalogMatches()` uses no global offset. This matches all current callers, though it changes the old implicit accumulation behavior for any future caller that neglects to zero `gl_norOffset`.
- **Legacy writer evidence:** the local published `v7.4.5` source confirms `Save_NOR_info` remains a raw in-place write at `0x7A0000`; `Loadfile2NOR` and startup call it, while delete-last erases only the newest S98 ROM and format erases S98 then clears RAM. `Save_info` erases a full 64-KiB S71 sector before writing raw catalog bytes. Thus an older writer can erase an active primary's recovery header while an active shadow remains.
- **Verified design counterexample:** current F-032 transaction copies prior catalog A to shadow, writes newer catalog B to primary, then retires A. If power is lost or retirement fails before any marker bit changes, both are active; source then treats `COMPLETE_UNRETIRED` as successful (`Save_NOR_info` returns true for every non-failed result). A later v7.4.5-style append writes S98 ROM first, then erases primary before programming a new raw table. If power fails after the erase and before the table write, the new reader sees a legacy blank primary and active shadow A. The scanner gives the blank primary zero consecutive matches while A can match the previous games; `NOR_CatalogChooseSource` selects A even though B had been the last fully committed catalog. This drops at least B's latest catalog entry from the visible NOR list. An older kernel cannot recover B because the only shadow copy was the prior generation.
- **Host model change/validation:** fixed an existing flawed unretired-retirement test setup so it now starts from old primary A, writes B, and fails retirement; assertions confirm active primary B and active shadow A. Added a deterministic counterexample that erases primary to its legacy-blank state and injects `0` versus `3` consecutive-match counts; it confirms the selector chooses stale A over previously committed B. `sh scripts/test-nor-catalog-recovery-host.sh` PASS. The match counts are model inputs; the test does not run the actual S98 MMIO scanner or flash commands.
- **Disposition:** likely Medium F-032 design defect for compatibility/recovery across older raw writers. The code cannot distinguish a stale active shadow after failed retirement from the desired backup for an interrupted legacy primary update. Returning failure on retirement error or retrying on the next new-firmware boot reduces the window but cannot cover power loss followed directly by a legacy boot. A safe redesign needs a current-generation shadow invariant or a narrowed, explicit downgrade guarantee; do not claim the current implementation is validated. 0x7C0000 remains unverified across undocumented revisions despite vendor map/datasheet geometry evidence. No hardware flash, release, commit, or push occurred.
- **Next:** decide against this F-032 integration unless a design can preserve a current recovery copy across every primary rewrite without sacrificing interruption recovery. If rejected, checkpoint the exact rejection and carefully remove only F-032 production integration while retaining the base header validation work, test evidence as appropriate, and all F-033–F-036 fixes. Then rebuild/run all focused suites and diff checks; continue F-022 polling/result review. Keep files/ untouched.

## 2026-10-16 15:32 UTC — F-032 recovery design needs downgrade-safe shadow invalidation

- **Phase/subsystem:** F-022 S71 NOR catalog durability. **Objective:** inspect the prior session's proposed helper before adding tests or touching production code.
- **Investigation:** verified `source/nor_catalog_recovery.h` exists and is complete as a pure helper; re-read `Save_info`, `Save_NOR_info`, `Read_NOR_info`, `NOR_PersistCatalog`, `Loadfile2NOR`, and the startup zero-catalog initialization. Confirmed the layout preserves the first 8,448 legacy catalog bytes and `Save_info` writes the whole sector before returning.
- **New compatibility risk (design flaw, not yet production defect):** if a successful new-firmware save leaves its valid shadow indefinitely, an older firmware can later erase/rewrite the primary in the legacy raw format. On the next new-firmware boot, “valid shadow beats legacy primary” would silently restore the stale catalog and hide games newly added by the older firmware. Preferring every legacy primary instead is unsafe after a power cut during the new primary rewrite. A one-way invalidation marker in the shadow, written only after verified primary commit, could resolve the ordinary downgrade path; host injection must cover interruption during invalidation and recovery from a valid shadow. Existing helper has no invalidation callback and therefore is not ready to test/integrate as-is.
- **Checkpoint direction:** user requested that the next checkpoint verify repository tidiness, completed fixes' commit/push state, cleanup of their temporary workfiles, then resume remaining audit. No commit, push, cleanup, production edit, or test occurred in this milestone. The existing mixed worktree and user-owned `files/` are preserved. ## 2026-10-16 15:40 UTC — S71 one-way marker command compatibility checked

- **Phase/subsystem:** F-022 S71 metadata recovery. **Objective:** determine whether a post-commit shadow invalidation marker can use commands supported by both source-level S71 paths without relying on undocumented repeated programming of the same flash word.
- **Primary documentation reviewed:** Spansion [S29GL-A datasheet](https://www.farnell.com/datasheets/1500802.pdf), §9.4.1, states standard single-word programming is supported, while programming the same word repeatedly without erase needs a modified method. Infineon [S29PL-J datasheet](https://www.infineon.com/assets/row/public/documents/10/49/infineon-s29pl-j-128--128--64--32-mbit-8-8-4-2m-x-16-bit-3-v-flash-with-enhanced-versatileio-datasheet-en.pdf?fileId=8ac78c8c7d0d8da4017d0ed5806154f0), §15.5, describes its four-cycle word program and 1->0-only behavior. This supports programming two *distinct* one-time halfword markers: active at the very end of a 64-byte header (last write), retirement in a separate still-erased halfword. Header 8,448-byte payload + 64-byte header = 8,512 bytes, still a multiple of the existing 32-byte GL write-buffer transfer unit.
- **Design candidate, not code:** shadow active only if its immutable header/data CRC validates, its active marker matches, and retirement marker remains `0xFFFF`. After primary candidate plus header verify, program the distinct retirement halfword to zero; any changed/non-erased retirement value makes the shadow ineligible. Primary-valid always outranks shadow. If primary is invalid, an active shadow is recoverable; if shadow is retired and primary is legacy, use primary. Put the active marker at the last halfword so a partial shadow write cannot make the copy valid before its payload/header. This avoids a second program of one word and ordinary successful downgrade would see the retired shadow.
- **Residual ambiguity:** if power loss or a program failure occurs before *any* bit of the retirement marker changes, the shadow remains valid. A later older firmware can rewrite the raw primary, which is indistinguishable from an interrupted new-firmware primary write to a new reader. Thus source docs support the marker command, but do not yet prove a fully downgrade-safe transaction under every failed retirement. Need test and assess whether another persistent state signal or semantic catalog validation can resolve it; do not integrate or claim full compatibility yet.
- **Next:** create the host model with distinct marker words and inject cuts before/during/after active and retire programming, shadow/primary erase and write, then include an older-firmware raw rewrite after each state. If the model exposes a remaining ambiguity that cannot be safely resolved, document F-032 as not fixed rather than accepting a silent stale catalog.

## 2026-10-16 15:27 UTC — F-032 S71 geometry reconciled; shadow-sector design is viable to test

- **Phase/subsystem:** F-022 NOR metadata persistence. **Objective:** identify whether a second S71 catalog sector can be justified from the actual cartridge allocation and supported flash-family geometry.
- **Vendor board evidence:** fetched the public `EZ-FLASH OMEGA DE DOCUMENT.pdf` from the upstream `ez-flash/omega-de-kernel` repository. Its S71GL064A08 NOR allocation maps the bootloader at 16-bit addresses `0x000000–0x007FFF`, kernel at `0x020000–<0x120000` (maximum 2 MiB), save metadata at `0x3C8000–0x3CFFFF`, NOR catalog at `0x3D0000–0x3D7FFF`, and settings at `0x3D8000–0x3DFFFF`. The system memory map names the full 64-Mbit S71 NOR. There is no named allocation at word address `0x3E0000` (byte offset `0x7C0000`); this is an unassigned region in the vendor allocation table, not proof that other undocumented firmware never uses it.
- **Primary geometry evidence:** the Infineon/Spansion S29GL-A datasheet documents 64-Mbit uniform and boot-sector variants and lists byte offsets `0x7A0000–0x7AFFFF`, `0x7B0000–0x7BFFFF`, and `0x7C0000–0x7CFFFF` as 64-KiB sectors (x16 word addresses `0x3D0000`, `0x3D8000`, and `0x3E0000`). The official Infineon S29PL-J PL064J table likewise maps x16 `0x3E0000–0x3E7FFF` to one 32-Kword/64-KiB sector, corresponding to byte offset `0x7C0000`. Thus the candidate sector geometry is consistent with both `0x2202` PL064J and the GL064 family code path; the exact BOM for every cartridge revision remains a caveat.
- **Source reconciliation:** `source/ez_define.h` places save/NOR/settings metadata at byte offsets `0x790000`, `0x7A0000`, `0x7B0000`. `Save_info` erases one full 64-KiB sector and `Save_NOR_info` writes the raw `FM_NOR_FS[MAX_NOR]` array in-place. `Read_NOR_info` loads only that raw array. `FM_NOR_FS` is 132 bytes under the target ABI; 64 entries occupy 8,448 bytes, and `pReadCache` has 128 KiB for temporary staging. No source S71 access to `0x7C0000` was found. `GetFileListFromNor` returns zero on a first-entry catalog/title mismatch; startup then clears and rewrites the catalog, so there is no existing persistent recovery attempt before that destructive normalization.
- **Conclusion:** F-032 remains a source-verified single-copy interruption risk, but the available evidence now supports a host-testable backward-compatible shadow-copy design using the unassigned 64-KiB sector at `0x7C0000`. Keep the legacy catalog bytes at `0x7A0000` for existing readers; append a fixed-size integrity header after the 8,448-byte array, write/verify the shadow before erasing the primary, and make boot choose a valid primary or recover a valid shadow. The firmware update must preserve old callers, avoid a large EWRAM allocation by using the existing `pReadCache`, and fail before primary erase if shadow creation fails. This is a proposed design, not implemented or hardware-validated. Before coding, build a pure host model that fault-injects failure during shadow erase/program, primary erase/program/header, and reboot selection; only then integrate it.
- **Other evidence:** the official board document and the manufacturer datasheets are linked in the continuation handoff; the official board PDF is in `https://github.com/ez-flash/omega-de-kernel/blob/main/EZ-FLASH%20OMEGA%20DE%20DOCUMENT.pdf`, the vendor source tree. Manufacturer PDFs: [S29GL-A datasheet](https://www.farnell.com/datasheets/1500802.pdf) and [S29PL-J datasheet](https://www.infineon.com/dgdl/Infineon-S29PL-J_128-_128-_64-_32-MBIT_%288_8_4_2M_X_16_BIT%29_3_V_FLASH_WITH_ENHANCED_VERSATILEIO-DataSheet-v07_00-EN.pdf?fileId=8ac78c8c7d0d8da4017d0ed5806154f0). The 2021 vendor PDF is direct board documentation from the upstream vendor repository; no hardware was written and no build/test was run in this milestone.
- **Next:** implement the pure catalog slot/header validation and injectable commit ordering, add power-cut regression cases, then integrate into `Save_NOR_info`/`Read_NOR_info` only if the host model proves every pre-primary failure preserves a recoverable copy. Preserve `files/`; do not publish or modify the v7.4.5 release.

## 2026-10-16 15:12 UTC — PPB and callback fixes validated; catalog interruption risk confirmed

- **Phase/subsystem:** F-022 NOR status and metadata persistence. **Objective:** validate PPB status handling and interrupt-state preservation, then trace persistent catalog behavior after a failed in-place update.
- **F-030 validation:** the PPB All Erase path now uses paired DQ6/DQ5 polling at the sector-zero address in the bank selected by its command entry, verifies DQ0=1, and exits PPB mode on all paths. `source/nor_flash_status.h` provides the pure classifier and the host regression covers busy, DQ5-confirmation, stable completion, and erased/programmed DQ0. Official procedure and the required 500 microsecond startup delay are linked in the prior 15:06 entry. The old fixed `0x15000` read loop and its unexamined `+0x5C0000` address are removed.
- **F-031 behavior and correction:** BIOS SWI 5 (`VBlankIntrWait`) invokes `IntrWait`; the available [GBA BIOS API reference](https://docs.rs/gba/latest/gba/bios/fn.IntrWait.html) says that service forces IME on. `Chip_Erase` saves the caller's IME, disables it, then calls `Wait_Flash_Operation` with a callback that uses VBlankIntrWait. Before correction, the poller returned from callbacks without restoring IME. It now saves REG_IME before each callback and restores it after return. Target disassembly confirms the load/callback/store sequence at both progress sites in IWRAM `Wait_Flash_Operation` (0x03000120). Severity is **Low**: current registered IRQs are VBlank and Timer1; earlier linked review established the VBlank zero-handler path does not fetch its default text handler, and the Timer1 call chain is in IWRAM. No unsafe callback was reproduced; the fix preserves the caller's documented mask.
- **F-032, likely Medium — single-copy NOR catalog can be torn:** `NOR_CommitCatalogSlot` provides only an in-memory backup. Its callback calls `Save_NOR_info`, which invokes `Save_info` at `NOR_info_offset` (0x7A0000): erase and verify a 64 KiB sector, then program catalog words/buffers in that same sector. There is no alternate catalog copy, generation marker, or checksum in the inspected read/write path. On startup `Read_NOR_info` copies that sector directly to `pNorFS`, then `GetFileListFromNor` relies on the stored game title and size records while scanning S98 ROM headers. A power interruption after S71 erase can therefore make some/all installed NOR titles unlistable even though the S98 ROM bytes remain. This is a source-trace likelihood, not a power-cut reproduction. Severity Medium due to a low-frequency event affecting a major feature. A compatible fix needs verified S71 sector geometry and legacy migration/recovery; no speculative layout change was made.
- **Caller review so far:** `FormatNor` distinguishes cancel/success/failure and the only UI caller checks failure code 2; NOR delete checks `Block_Erase`; install checks block erase, buffered programming, PPB, and catalog-save status; settings/save-marker callers checked by `rg` also branch on Save_* failures. `Set_saveMODE` results are ignored in two first-game install branches but that function displays its own flash error; the in-repository effect of its SET_info field is not established, so do not yet classify this as a defect.
- **Validation:** after both fixes, `make -B`, all four host scripts (`test-nor-flash-status`, `test-nor-catalog-transaction`, `test-launcher-navigation`, `test-fatfs-long-name`), and `git diff --check` pass. The target ELF disassembly verifies the callback IME restoration and delay helper. A fresh Cppcheck run was not available: even `cppcheck --version` failed at sandbox startup with `bwrap: No permissions to create new namespace`; prior targeted Cppcheck results remain recorded in the earlier journal. No hardware flash or S71/S98 runtime test occurred.
- **Next:** inspect S71 geometry at the exact metadata offsets and all startup/corruption handling; evaluate a backward-compatible recovery strategy for F-032 without writing flash. Finish the complete `Save_*`/NOR result propagation scan and the unbounded DQ6 timeout review. Preserve user files and the mixed uncommitted audit worktree.

There is no software wall-clock PPB limit; do not claim a bounded response time.

The available GBA BIOS API reference documents that IntrWait forces IME on. The poller invokes callbacks without saving/restoring IME, so after a progress callback the ongoing chip-erase polling runs with IME enabled even though the caller intentionally disabled it. This is a source/state-trace finding; impact depends on remaining IRQ handlers during S98 erase. Earlier F-022 review moved the Timer1 audio handler's complete call chain to IWRAM and established the VBlank default path runs in the S71/kernel device; confirm all currently enabled IRQ paths before concluding the mask is necessary. Next narrow change is to preserve/restore REG_IME around each optional callback in `Wait_Flash_Operation`.
- **Catalog risk remains:** RAM rollback is verified by the host test, but `Save_info` erases a 64 KiB S71 metadata sector then programs in place. A power interruption or programming failure can leave persistent metadata blank/torn; no dual-slot recovery was found. A durable fix needs a separate transactional storage design and power-loss tests.

FatFs instrumentation reports 16 files -> 17 entries and 96 files -> 97 entries for one LFN fallback, and zero scans for complete indexed LFNs. No host test exercises memory-mapped S98 hardware; no physical test occurred.
- **Likely finding F-030, Medium — All-PPB erase result is ignored:** `source/NORflash_OP.c::Loadfile2NOR` enters PPB mode (`AA/55/C0`), issues All Erase (`80/30`), performs a fixed `0x15000` reads from `FlashBase_S98+0x5C0000`, exits (`90/00`), and continues without interpreting status or checking PPB status DQ0. The kernel admits S98 ID `0x223D`, which the official [Infineon S29WS512P datasheet](https://www.infineon.com/assets/row/public/documents/10/49/infineon-s29ws512p-s29ws256p-s29ws128p-512-256-128-mb-32-16-8-m-x-16-bit-1.8-v-simultaneous-read-write-flash-datasheet-en.pdf) identifies as WS512P. Its PPB algorithm specifies an initial 500 microsecond wait, paired reads and DQ6/DQ5 polling, status read at a sector address with DQ0=1 meaning unprotected, and a required PPB command-set exit. It also states that a locked PPB may time out without changing state. The source read at +0x5C0000 is not decoded as status, so elapsed reads alone cannot distinguish success/failure. Severity and user impact remain likely rather than reproduced: downstream block erase/write checks may reject a protected target, but may hang or enter a confusing command state if the PPB command is still active.
- **Evidence limitations:** the command entry at `FlashBase_S98+0x555*2` selects the bank represented by the current mapped base; the manufacturer algorithm's status reads are tied to the selected bank/sector. Confirm this address relationship with the kernel page-register behavior before modifying the polling address. The device datasheet does not provide a clearly identified software wall-clock maximum for PPB erase, so do not invent a timeout. Existing `Wait_Flash_Operation` also has an unbounded DQ6 loop; normal device status eventually terminates it, but a permanently toggling bus can block the UI.
- **Catalog persistence risk:** `NOR_CommitCatalogSlot` and its host test verify rollback only in RAM. `Save_info` first erases a 64 KiB S71 sector at the catalog offset, then programs in place. A power interruption or program error after the erase can leave persistent NOR metadata blank/torn; RAM rollback cannot recover it. No redundant-sector recovery mechanism was found in the examined call path. This requires a separate design/recovery review, not an in-memory-only fix.
- **Next:** implement and test a pure PPB status classifier; only change `Loadfile2NOR` if source mapping and PPB exit/error handling are consistent with the official algorithm. Rebuild, rerun all relevant host suites, inspect compiled output, and then review every `Wait_Flash_Operation` and Save_* caller. Do not flash hardware, guess S98 `0x020`, or claim physical validation.

## 2026-10-16 14:39 UTC — F-022: S98 ID-reader leaves flash in autoselect mode

- **Phase/subsystem:** NOR flash protocol and error exits. **Objective:** resume F-022 by checking PPB erase and catalog rollback behavior, then verify all flash-command entry/exit paths and callers.
- **Source examined:** `source/Ezcard_OP.c::Read_S98NOR_ID`, `Read_S71NOR_ID`, `Save_info`; `source/NORflash_OP.c::Loadfile2NOR`, `Block_Erase`, `NOR_CommitCatalogSlot`, `FormatNor`, and status writers; `source/ezkernelnew.c::Boot_NOR_game` and NOR-install result handling; `source/nor_flash_status.h`; `source/nor_catalog_transaction.h`; both NOR host tests/scripts.
- **Verified finding F-029, Medium — missing autoselect reset on S98 ID read:** `Read_S98NOR_ID` writes `F0, AA/55, 90`, reads X0E, then returns without a reset. The sole caller is `Loadfile2NOR`; unsupported ID and file-open/validation/seek/read failures can exit before its later `F0` before PPB erase. The error UI returns to the launcher, and `Boot_NOR_game` does not issue an S98 reset before reading the NOR title. Therefore a failed install can leave S98 in autoselect mode, where subsequent reads are not array data. Source trace establishes the state leak; hardware consequence is inferred and not physically reproduced.
- **Primary reference:** Infineon's official [S29WS512P datasheet](https://www.infineon.com/assets/row/public/documents/10/49/infineon-s29ws512p-s29ws256p-s29ws128p-512-256-128-mb-32-16-8-m-x-16-bit-1.8-v-simultaneous-read-write-flash-datasheet-en.pdf) maps X0E `0x223D` to WS512P and specifies reset to leave autoselect and return to array-read mode. The source's ID gate accepts only `0x223D`; this finding does not establish S98 `020` compatibility.
- **Fix plan:** add the matching `F0` reset immediately after capturing the ID in `Read_S98NOR_ID`. This restores the device state for every caller return path. No host unit test directly exercises MMIO; validate with a fresh target build, all existing host NOR/catalog/navigation/FatFs tests, whitespace, and review of ID-reader calls and error exits. Do not flash hardware.
- **PPB review finding, likely:** the All-PPB Erase command sequence matches the x16 S29GL064N command table and is entered only after the S98 ID gate, but it waits for a constant `0x15000` volatile read count at `FlashBase_S98+0x5C0000`, ignores status and PPB state, then exits the command set and continues installation. The official WS512P flow instead polls DQ6/DQ5 at `SA0`, checks PPB status DQ0, and requires the PPB command-set exit; its notes also allow the lock bit to cause PPB erase to time out. The current command acceptance and page/bank mapping of the poll address need deeper source/datasheet reconciliation before changing it. An erase failure of a protected destination is later detected by `Block_Erase` verification, but that does not prove the PPB operation is correct.
- **Catalog transaction review:** `NOR_CommitCatalogSlot` backs up the in-RAM slot, exposes the candidate to `Save_NOR_info`, and restores the slot if the callback returns failure. Its host test covers callback success/failure. This does not roll back the physical S71 metadata sector after `Save_info` has erased it; interrupted/failed metadata updates can still leave a torn on-flash catalog. Existing `Loadfile2NOR` caller reports failure (return 1); further caller-state review remains part of this audit.
- **Commands/evidence:** `rg` call-site search, focused `sed` reads of implementation/callers, `git log -S'0x5C0000'`, and official Infineon S29WS512P and S29GL064N datasheet review. No code changed, and no build/tests were run in this investigation checkpoint. Release ID/tag/asset verification remains recorded in the 14:35 entry.
- **Next:** implement F-029's one-line array-mode reset, then build and run all four focused host scripts (`test-nor-flash-status-host.sh`, `test-nor-catalog-transaction-host.sh`, `test-launcher-navigation-host.sh`, `test-fatfs-long-name-host.sh`) plus `git diff --check`. Inspect the full F-022 diff and confirm no ignored low-level status returns. Keep PPB status semantics, no finite timeout, S98 `020` mapping, and torn S71 metadata open unless evidence and a safe implementation emerge.

## 2026-10-16 14:04 UTC — Remove repeated full-directory scans from normal thumbnail lookup

- **Phase/subsystem:** Launcher SD browser; indexed filename and artwork lookup.
- **Verified finding, Medium:** `Launcher_LoadThumbDataForIndex` previously reopened and scanned the current directory through `Launcher_ResolveDirectoryEntry` for every thumbnailed file/folder, even though the directory index already retained that entry's complete LFN. The resolver scans to the end to reject ambiguous SFN/LFN matches. With the three-slot cache, this repeated a folder-sized scan for cache misses as navigation advanced; browsing N ordinary entries could therefore add O(N²) directory-entry visits. This is independent from the one-time artwork parent-path canonicalization cache.
- **Fix:** new `source/launcher_indexed_name.h` copies the stored filename directly unless `FM_ENTRY_ALIAS_DISPLAY` marks it clipped at the 99-byte index limit. Only clipped names call the existing ambiguity-checked alias resolver, and its callback verifies file-vs-directory type. `Launcher_LoadThumbDataForIndex` applies this to both file and folder entries. Recent/favourite paths already resolve full names and remain unchanged.
- **Reproducible host evidence:** `tests/fatfs-long-name-host.c` builds separate 16-file and 96-file FatFs directories. Resolver metrics show a single clipped-name fallback examines 17 and 97 entries respectively, while all ordinary complete indexed LFNs in both folders make zero resolver calls/visits. The test also verifies that a >99-byte LFN is still recovered exactly through the alias resolver. Output: `indexed-name resolver scans: 16 files -> 17 entries; 96 files -> 97 entries; complete indexed LFNs -> 0 scans`.
- **Validation:** `sh scripts/test-fatfs-long-name-host.sh` PASS; target `make -B` PASS and linked `omega-de-ds-style-multisystem-kernel.gba`; `git diff --check` PASS. No physical SD/hardware latency measurement was possible. This confirms removal of the repeated work for untruncated names; individually selected filenames clipped beyond 99 bytes still require one O(N) ambiguity check.

## 2026-10-16 12:51 UTC — PPB erase sequence identified; fixed wait remains unverified

- **Objective:** map every S98 flash command path while reviewing F-022 status/error coverage.
- **Source evidence:** `source/NORflash_OP.c::Loadfile2NOR`'s “erase all BBP” sequence enters protection command mode with `AA @ 0x555`, `55 @ 0x2AA`, `C0 @ 0x555`, then issues `80 @ 0`, `30 @ 0`; it polls via exactly `0x15000` volatile reads of `FlashBase_S98+0x5C0000`, then exits with `90 @ 0`, `00 @ 0`. The operation purpose is now identified as **All PPB Erase** (the source comment's “BBP” is a typo), not an ordinary array-sector erase.
- **Primary corroboration:** Infineon's official [S29GL064N datasheet](https://www.infineon.com/assets/row/public/documents/10/49/infineon-s29gl064n-s29gl032n-64-mbit-32-mbit-3-v-page-mode-mirrorbit-flash-datasheet-en.pdf) x16 sector-protection table specifies the same PPB entry (`AA/55/C0`), All PPB Erase (`80` then `30` at address 0), and exit (`90`, then `00`). It says DQ6 toggles during PPB erasure and PPBs erase in parallel; its PPB algorithm checks DQ5/DQ6 and verifies PPB status. The command sequence is therefore consistent with this documented family.
- **Revised assessment:** the previous “purpose unknown” assessment is superseded by the evidence above, but the exact S98 MCP/revision and whether every Omega DE variant follows this status protocol remain unverified. Current code does not inspect status bits or verify cleared PPB state; it uses a fixed iteration delay and exits the command set afterward. This is a likely reliability defect in the PPB completion path, not yet safe to change without mapping the board's actual part and the meaning/addressing of status reads for the supported revisions. A fixed poll count has no documented time bound.
- **Other path review:** sector/block erase, chip erase, word programming, buffered programming, and S71 metadata erase/program are routed through `Wait_Flash_Operation`, followed by exact erased/data verification where applicable. That helper handles DQ6 busy, DQ5 confirmation, buffered DQ1 abort recovery, and stable readback mismatch, but every busy loop remains unbounded. The existing host test exercises only the pure classifier; no flash bus/operation integration is simulated. `Loadfile2NOR` propagates block/program failures and commits the catalog only after those writes and the metadata save call.
- **Validation:** no source changed and no build/test was run for this source/documentation review. Hardware PPB behavior is unavailable; no physical flash operation was performed.
- **Next:** establish the S98 part/revision and supported PPB status/read-address rules from authoritative Omega DE sources; then decide whether replacing the fixed delay with bounded, status-aware PPB handling is safe. Separately inspect failure behavior if `Save_NOR_info` returns false after the new catalog slot was copied into RAM. Run target build and the focused NOR-status host test after any justified fix. Do not select an arbitrary timeout.

## 2026-10-16 12:46 UTC — S71 default VBlank callback hazard disproved

- **Correction to earlier 02:21 likely-defect entry:** the table's default handler pointer is `dummy` in ROM (`0x0801fc7d` in the current audit ELF), but `irqInit` also sets every default table mask to zero. `IntrMain` checks the mask and branches to `no_handler` when it is zero, before loading/calling the handler pointer. The old conclusion inferred reachability from the pointer alone and was wrong; retain the history and mark this hypothesis disproved.
- **Repository/linked evidence:** `rg -n "irqSet\\(" source` finds only `irqSet(IRQ_TIMER1, UIAudio_Timer1IRQ)` at `source/ezkernelnew.c:1508`; main enables VBlank but never registers a VBlank handler. Current `irqInit` disassembly at `0x0801FCDC` writes `0x0801FC7D` and mask `0` into the default entries in `IntrTable` (`0x03005BF8`). Current `IntrMain` disassembly at `0x030038B4` has `cmp r0,#0; beq no_handler` before `ldr r2,[r2]` at `0x03003910`. Thus VBlank during S71 command mode only reaches the IWRAM dispatcher/no-handler path and BIOS wait-flag update; it does not fetch the default callback from S71.
- **Primary corroboration:** devkitPro libgba [`irqInit`](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/devkitPro/libgba/master/src/interrupt.c) initializes each handler to `dummy` and mask to zero; [`IntrMain`](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/devkitPro/libgba/master/src/InterruptDispatcher.s) tests mask zero and returns through `no_handler` before the handler load/call. The official [devkitPro BIOS dumper example](https://github.com/devkitPro/gba-examples/blob/master/utility/biosdumper/source/biosdumper.c) says the default dispatcher handles BIOS wait flags and no VBlank handler is required.
- **Interrupt residue:** Timer1 is the only registered IRQ handler and the audited F-022 commit places `UIAudio_Timer1IRQ`, `UIAudio_StopFromTimer`, and `UIAudio_StopHardware` in IWRAM. `Save_info` and `Read_S71NOR_ID` are IWRAM code; `Read_S71NOR_ID` is called only from `Save_info`. No VBlank masking change is justified by the default-handler theory. This is static/source/link evidence; no physical flash test was performed.
- **Next:** continue F-022's actual NOR correctness review, starting with `source/nor_flash_status.h`, `source/Ezcard_OP.c::Wait_Flash_Operation/Save_info`, `source/NORflash_OP.c` writers/callers, and `tests/nor-flash-status-host.c`. Reassess DQ status handling, complete operation coverage, catalog/result propagation, and the documented unknown S71 part/timeout bound. Do not introduce arbitrary timeouts or alter the published v7.4.5 release.

## 2026-10-16 11:37 UTC — F-027/F-028 causes verified from v7.4.4 source paths

- **Objective:** trace the two user-reported regressions to implementation causes before editing, and retain the newly requested browser-wrap feature as a later independent task.
- **F-027, High, verified source defect with user-reported manifestation:** `Makefile` defines `__sp_usr=0x0203F000`; `source/reset.s` loads it for both reset paths. The setting reads `gl_toggle_reset=0` as Off/default (`source/ezkernelnew.c:5469-5472`); direct game boot passes that value to `SetRompageWithHardReset` (`:16775-16778`). In `source/Ezcard_OP.c:348-357`, `bootmode==0` adds `RESET_EWRAM` and calls `RegisterRamReset`, then falls through to `SoftReset_now`. The SWI clears EWRAM, including the current C stack, so the later function epilogue/return path is invalid. Official libgba documents `RESET_EWRAM` as “Clear 256K on-board WRAM” and `RegisterRamReset` as resetting registers and RAM: [devkitPro libgba system-call header](https://github.com/devkitPro/libgba/blob/master/include/gba_systemcalls.h). This regression is attributable to combining the F-026 EWRAM stack relocation with the pre-existing bootmode-0 EWRAM-clear behavior. No build/hardware reproduction has yet been performed in this session.
- **F-028, Low, verified UI-path defect with user-reported manifestation:** folder entry at `source/ezkernelnew.c:15862-15882` appends `Launcher_FolderIdentityName(...)` (the SFN alias) into `currentpath` to keep identity/path operations exact. The title-bar path at `:5725-5729` calls `Launcher_GetCurrentFolderLabel`; that function at `:7606-7620` returns the final raw component of `currentpath`. It therefore displays the SFN alias directly. Preserve the identity path and resolve only the final directory component back to its LFN for display.
- **Planned corrections:** F-027 will use a no-return IWRAM trampoline that disables IRQ delivery, moves SP to the reserved IWRAM reset scratch address, invokes `RegisterRamReset` with the existing reset flags, and branches to `SoftReset_now`; this retains the intended EWRAM clear without returning through erased EWRAM stack frames. F-028 will resolve/cache the final current-folder component as an LFN for the title bar while leaving `currentpath` alias-based for filesystem operations.
- **Feature sequencing:** after these fixes are built and reviewed, implement the requested browser wraparound option separately. Default it Off; enabled navigation will cycle both last-to-first and first-to-last. It belongs in Interface settings and must cover list, list-art, horizontal, vertical, and mixed folder/file navigation.
- **Next:** implement the two fixes; validate with target `make`, reset-symbol/disassembly/stack-address inspection, an extended in-memory FatFs directory-alias fixture, existing `sh scripts/test-nor-flash-status-host.sh`, and `git diff --check`. ### F-022 adversarial review reopened S71 metadata IRQ safety (2026-10-16 02:21 UTC)

- **Discovery:** linked `irqInit` disassembly initializes the handler table at `0x03005be0` with default handler address `0x0801f875` (Thumb `dummy` in S71 `.text`); the IRQ dispatcher `IntrMain` itself is IWRAM. `source/ezkernelnew.c` enables VBlank and registers only Timer1 explicitly; Timer1's custom handler is IWRAM. Earlier handoff wording that called the default VBlank handler IWRAM was wrong and is corrected here.
- **Likely defect, verification in progress:** `Save_info` runs S71 ID/erase/program command sequences from IWRAM, but current source review has not found VBlank masked around those sequences. If VBlank dispatches while S71 is in ID or embedded busy mode, the default handler fetches its instructions from the same S71 device. This could corrupt execution or stall metadata writes. The separate-chip justification only covers moving S98 operations to S71 `.text`; it does not protect S71 from its own default ROM callback.
- **Investigation scope:** enumerate all `FlashBase_S71` command/ID paths and `Save_info` callers; inspect all `REG_IE`, `irqDisable`, and `REG_IME` state changes; confirm whether the command-mode intervals can be interrupted; then make the narrowest fix that masks only unsafe S71-text IRQ callbacks and restores the caller's prior interrupt state on every exit. Timer1 can remain enabled because its entire handler is IWRAM, pending full call-chain review.
- **Validation required:** add/adjust a host-testable helper only if the logic is separable; target build and ELF/disassembly review must prove interrupt-mask save/restore is correct and all remaining enabled IRQ paths execute from safe memory. Physical cartridge behavior remains untested.
- **Current code change status:** no fix for this newly identified hazard yet. The just-validated S98 routine relocation remains uncommitted. Do not commit/push until this review is resolved or explicitly scoped as an open likely defect.
- **Next:** inspect `Read_S71NOR_ID`, every other S71 command path, all `Save_info` call contexts, and exact IRQ mask semantics before editing.

### F-022 recovered IWRAM stack margin and passed linked-placement review (2026-10-16 02:18 UTC)

- **Objective:** recover the 208-byte static-data-to-IRQ-stack gap exposed by full NOR readback while keeping S71 metadata flash routines safe during their own busy periods.
- **Change:** removed `IWRAM_CODE` from only S98-specific `Block_Erase` and `WriteFlash_with32word` in `source/NORflash_OP.c`; removed the matching declaration attribute from `source/NORflash_OP.h`. `Wait_Flash_Operation`, `Verify_Flash_Erased`, `Verify_Flash_Data`, `Save_info`, IRQ dispatcher, and Timer1 audio handler remain in IWRAM. The official board map identifies S71 kernel/bootloader and S98 game NOR as separate devices, so these two S98 routines and their linker veneers execute from S71 while S98 is busy.
- **Linked evidence:** target ELF places `Block_Erase` at `0x08001a3a` and `WriteFlash_with32word` at `0x08001e44` in `.text`; polling/readback helpers at `0x03000120`, `0x030001de`, and `0x03000208`; `Save_info` at `0x03000780`; Timer1 handler at `0x030010ac`; and `IntrMain` at `0x0300389c`. `irqInit` initializes all vector slots to the S71 `.text` default handler at `0x0801f875`; source has only Timer1 registration and enables Timer1 and VBlank. Static image/data ends at `0x03007be8`, with `__sp_irq=0x03007fa0`, leaving a 952-byte address gap. This is linker headroom, not a measured runtime high-water mark. Full function disassembly confirms relocated routines call the IWRAM poll/readback helpers via S71 interworking stubs; no call into the busy S98 window was found.

### F-022 IWRAM stack margin fell to 208 bytes; recover through S98 code relocation (2026-10-16 02:15 UTC)

### F-022 complete erase/program readback added (2026-10-16 02:13 UTC)

- **Objective:** remove false-success cases where one status word happened to match even though a sector, write buffer, or chip was not fully correct.
- **Changes:** added IWRAM `Verify_Flash_Erased` and `Verify_Flash_Data`; sector erase now scans each special 32-KiB subrange or the normal 128-KiB range; after each 16-word buffered program, all 16 words are compared with the source buffer; S71 metadata buffered writes receive the same complete compare. `Chip_Erase` now scans all eight 8-MiB S98 windows selected using the established `gl_currentpage + page_index*0x1000` mapping and returns failure if any word is not `0xFFFF`. It restores the original ROM page and IME on both success and mismatch; progress refresh runs every MiB during the scan.
- **Evidence basis:** `source/ezkernelnew.c` initializes `gl_currentpage` to `0x8002`; `source/NORflash_OP.c` uses 8-MiB windows and increments the page register by `0x1000`, with a 64-MiB NOR capacity. The official board document identifies 512-Mbit S98 NOR. The current full-range scan is a direct readback verification after the chip status routine has returned to read mode; no write/erase is performed by the scan.
- **Validation:** pending. No build/test/ELF placement check has been run since these latest range-scan changes; no physical cartridge flash/test.
- **Next:** run host NOR status regression and target build, inspect new IWRAM size/IRQ stack margin and helper disassembly, verify the chip erase loop maps/restores all banks, and run `git diff --check`. Do not commit until checks complete.

### F-022 first integration build and IWRAM placement pass; expand readback coverage (2026-10-16 02:10 UTC)

- **Validation so far:** `make` passed after two compile issues were corrected: the shared poll API now takes `vu16 *` pointers (rather than integer addresses) to match `FlashBase_S71`, and `Show_error_num` has a forward declaration before its first new call. Disassembly shows the pure status classifiers are inlined; the helper's only call path is its optional indirect callback (an IWRAM `bx r3` at `0x030001dc`). S71 paths pass a null callback. Static IWRAM/data ends at `0x03007de0`; `__sp_irq=0x03007fa0`, leaving 448 bytes before the IRQ stack top. This is a narrower margin than before; no runtime IRQ stack high-water was measured.

Chip erase checks only the first word before `FormatNor` clears the catalog. These can falsely accept protected/ignored or partial operations if the sampled data happened to match the expected word.
