### F-022 status failures propagated through NOR and metadata callers (2026-10-16 02:06 UTC)

- **Objective:** integrate the host-tested status decoder and ensure failures reach callers before they commit catalog state or report success.
- **Code changes:** added IWRAM `Wait_Flash_Operation` in `source/Ezcard_OP.c`, using the pure classifier, valid poll addresses, DQ5 confirm-pair logic, the required `AA/55/F0` write-buffer-abort reset, standard `F0` reset for failure, and two exact stable reads at the target word. It supports an optional progress callback. S71 `Save_info` passes no callback so its polling/reset path remains in IWRAM; S98 chip erase passes a progress callback whose code resides on the separate S71/kernel chip documented by the official board PDF.
- **Result propagation:** `Block_Erase`, `Chip_Erase`, `FormatNor`, `WriteFlash`, `WriteFlash_with32word`, and `Save_info`/metadata wrappers now return status. `Loadfile2NOR` stops on erase/program failure and skips catalog commit; `FormatNor` clears RAM catalog only after the status helper reports success; NOR deletion reports erase failure; settings and save-marker callers check results; save-marker creation failure prevents game startup.
- **User-visible handling:** reuses localized generic save error 3 for flash metadata/settings failures. `FormatNor` distinguishes success (1), cancellation (0), and flash failure (2), so cancel does not show an error.
- **Residual risks identified before validation:** the busy loop remains unbounded if DQ6 toggles forever without DQ5; a defensible cross-revision time limit is still absent. Erase operation readback checks only one word, and chip erase checks only the first word; protected/ignored erase can still look successful when those sampled words were already erased. Buffered programming verifies only the final word, not all 16. In-place S71 metadata failure can leave the one metadata sector partially erased/programmed; failure is now reported but not rolled back. The S98 `020` ID remains unverified.
- **Validation status:** the classifier test passed before integration. The integration edit has not yet been built or tested; map/disassembly inspection is required because the shared poller must be IWRAM-resident and the S71 path must not call back into S71 `.text`. No physical flash operation.

### F-022 pure NOR status decoder added and host-tested (2026-10-16 02:00 UTC)

### F-022 reset protocol confirmed; NOR status fix implementation checkpoint (2026-10-16 01:56 UTC)

- **Objective:** verify the mandatory device reset sequence after DQ5/DQ1 status errors, inspect every writer caller before changing return contracts, and checkpoint the planned implementation.
- **Primary reset evidence:** S29WS512P's official Infineon datasheet gives the write-buffer abort reset as `AA` at word address `0x555`, `55` at `0x2AA`, then `F0` at `0x555`; it states a standard `F0` reset does not clear DQ1 abort. S29GL-A's vendor datasheet gives the same sequence and requires `F0` after DQ5 failure. On this x16 byte-addressed map those unlock addresses are `0xAAA`, `0x554`, and `0xAAA`. Sources: [S29WS512P datasheet, write-buffer sequence and abort status](https://www.infineon.com/assets/row/public/documents/10/49/infineon-s29ws512p-s29ws256p-s29ws128p-512-256-128-mb-32-16-8-m-x-16-bit-1.8-v-simultaneous-read-write-flash-datasheet-en.pdf), [S29GL-A datasheet, write-buffer reset sequence](https://docs.rs-online.com/9812/0900766b80a27efb.pdf).
- **Caller review:** `Loadfile2NOR` calls sector erase and buffered program before catalog commit; `FormatNor` clears in-memory catalog after chip erase; the latest-title delete path ignores `Block_Erase` result; `Save_info` is wrapped by save/NOR/settings writers, with call sites in `source/ezkernelnew.c`, `source/setwindow.c`, and `source/setwindow2.c` currently ignoring results. Recovery-marker set/clear and settings/NOR metadata durability therefore need explicit caller behavior, not only a lower-level return value.
- **Implementation plan:** add host-pure status classification for DQ6 busy, DQ5 confirm/failure transition, DQ1 abort, and stable-array readback; test those state outcomes. Integrate in NOR helpers with the exact reset sequences and propagate results to all callers. Preserve current ID dispatch and keep S71 polling/reset code in IWRAM. Do not invent a timeout: the complete operation bound and S98 `020` ID remain unknown.
- **Validation:** official datasheet search/open review and `rg` plus source-range inspection only. No source modification, build, test, hardware operation, commit, or push occurred before this checkpoint.
- **Repository:** branch `feature/mirrored-artwork`; pushed `HEAD` and origin ref are `95e79891c8719f355f57bbbdf7332a5303d3befc`. Only audit docs are tracked modifications; preserve user-owned untracked `files/`.
- **Next:** implement/test the decoder first; then integrate and inspect the full failure handling/call chain. Hardware verification remains unavailable and will be reported as such.

### F-022 S98/S71 status protocol comparison; implementation staged as next task (2026-10-16 01:52 UTC)

- **Phase/subsystem:** main/S71 NOR flash protocol. **Objective:** determine which current polling choices agree with the manufacturer status protocol, whether the Rev B S98 `020` ID can be resolved from primary sources, and define the next safe implementation task.
- **Source examined:** local `source/NORflash_OP.c` (`Block_Erase`, `Chip_Erase`, `WriteFlash`, `WriteFlash_with32word`, `Loadfile2NOR`, `FormatNor`) and `source/Ezcard_OP.c` (`Read_S71NOR_ID`, `Read_S98NOR_ID`, `Save_info`); official EZ-FLASH public `NORflash_OP.c`; the already identified official Omega DE board PDF and S29WS512P/S29GL-A vendor datasheets.
- **Verified protocol match:** S29WS512P documents DQ7 polling at an address in the selected erase sector and buffer-program DQ7 polling at the last loaded word. The source uses the selected address in `Block_Erase` and last loaded word in `WriteFlash_with32word`. Full-word equality across successive reads also detects DQ6 toggling while the relevant part is actively busy, but it is not a complete success/error protocol.
- **Verified missing error handling / Medium:** S29WS512P and S29GL-A status documentation defines DQ5 timing-limit failures and DQ1 write-buffer abort. Their flowcharts require rereading/handling these conditions; DQ5 failure requires reset, and a buffer abort requires the write-buffer-abort-reset sequence. Current sector erase, word program, buffered program, chip erase, and S71 metadata loops have no DQ5/DQ1 result path or bound and return `void`. Persistent DQ6 toggling on a terminal failure therefore leaves the caller in an infinite loop; buffered writes do not issue the prescribed abort recovery. This is an evidenced liveness/error-propagation defect under the documented error states, not evidence that ordinary writes currently hang on cartridge hardware.
- **Likely false-success risk / Medium, hardware trigger unverified:** S29WS512P chip erase is documented as producing `FFFF` throughout memory on success; the datasheet also documents protected erase operations returning to stable array-read mode. `Chip_Erase` only checks that two full-word reads at the base compare equal, so a command rejected/protected and returned to array mode can look complete; `FormatNor` then clears the in-memory NOR catalog. No Omega DE protection configuration or physical occurrence was established. The same equality-only logic may also miss ignored word/buffer programming; do not claim a real cartridge manifestation without evidence.
- **Rev B suffix gap remains:** official EZ-FLASH release notes say FW7 adds support for S98 chips with `020` suffixes, but provide no model or X0E ID mapping. The public official kernel source (`https://github.com/ez-flash/omega-de-kernel/blob/main/source/NORflash_OP.c`) also tests only `norid == 0x223D`; that source may predate FW7 and does not establish the updated binary's behavior. Exact support in this branch is therefore **unverified**; do not turn it into an asserted incompatibility or add guessed ID dispatch.
- **Next implementation objective (checkpoint before production edits):** create a pure host-testable status-result decoder for busy/completion/DQ5/DQ1 outcomes and edge transitions from the vendor flowcharts; verify exact reset and buffer-abort-reset sequences across the two documented families; then integrate status returns through the flash helper and every relevant caller so failed operations cannot report success or update metadata. Keep same-chip busy call paths in RAM. Do not invent a wall-clock timeout until complete operation bounds and the S98 revision matrix are supported by primary evidence.
- **Commands/validation:** `git status --short`, `git rev-parse HEAD`, `git branch -vv`, source `rg`/`sed` inspection, and web searches/opens/finds against vendor documentation and official EZ-FLASH materials. Shell inspection initially failed because this container could not create the unprivileged `bwrap` namespace; the read-only command succeeded after the sandbox escalation approval. No code/build/test/hardware operation occurred in this protocol-comparison milestone.
- **Repository state:** branch `feature/mirrored-artwork`; local `HEAD` and `origin/feature/mirrored-artwork` both at pushed commit `95e79891c8719f355f57bbbdf7332a5303d3befc`; only the two audit ledgers are tracked modifications; user-owned untracked `files/` remains untouched. No commit or push for this new entry yet.
- **Next:** implement and host-test the pure status decoder as above, then integrate narrowly and run focused tests plus target build. If exact reset semantics or supported revisions prove too uncertain, stop the production edit and record the unresolved boundary with the supporting evidence.

### F-022 verified S71 busy-time IRQ execution path; correction planned (2026-10-16 01:40 UTC)

- **Objective:** determine whether the S71 metadata writer can be interrupted by code fetches from the same busy flash and checkpoint an evidence-backed fix before editing firmware.
- **Vendor evidence:** the Spansion S29GL-A data sheet (the flash family used by S71GL064A MCPs) says word-program completion returns to read mode; during embedded operation reads are status, while array reads from nonsuspended sectors are only permitted after program/erase suspend. Erase suspend is ignored for chip erase and applies to sector erase only. Sector erase is 0.5 s typical, up to 3.5 s excluding internal preprogramming. Source document (vendor data sheet hosted by Farnell): https://www.farnell.com/datasheets/1500802.pdf ; relevant sections: word program pp. 53–54, chip/sector erase pp. 58–60, suspend p. 59, erase times p. 86.
- **IRQ path corrected from prior hypothesis:** libgba `irqInit()` assigns `dummy` to unregistered slots, but `IntrMain` is assembly in `.iwram`; since no VBlank handler is registered, its default path clears/returns without calling a ROM handler. Thus routine VBlank IRQs alone are safe during S71 writes. `UIAudio_Init()` registers `UIAudio_Timer1IRQ` and enables Timer1; Timer1 is actively started as a sample-count completion interrupt by `UIAudio_PlayRaw`. The existing linked ELF places `UIAudio_Timer1IRQ` in `.text` at `0x08003560` (S71 kernel NOR). Reference sources: https://github.com/devkitPro/libgba/blob/master/src/interrupt.c and https://github.com/devkitPro/libgba/blob/master/src/InterruptDispatcher.s.
- **Verified defect / severity Medium:** `source/Ezcard_OP.c:339-401` runs `Save_info` from IWRAM but always issues an S71 sector erase command (`0x30`) and then programs metadata without masking IRQs. `source/ezkernelnew.c:9709-9720` has a concrete path: when the user presses B in save recovery, it starts the back sound, sets `discard_save`, then immediately erases/programs S71 to clear the recovery marker. With sounds enabled by default, Timer1 can fire during the typical 0.5-s sector erase and dispatch into `.text` while S71 is returning only status, likely causing a lockup. This is code + exact family data-sheet evidence; no physical cartridge reproduction was run.
- **Correction design:** move `UIAudio_Timer1IRQ`, `UIAudio_StopFromTimer`, and `UIAudio_StopHardware` into IWRAM as a closed call chain, preserving IRQ operation and avoiding masking interrupts over the long erase. The dispatcher is already IWRAM; no VBlank handler is registered. After editing, inspect linked sections/map and remaining IWRAM capacity. If compiler/linker introduces any call or literal fetch from `.text`, revise the chain rather than assuming the annotation was sufficient.
- **Repository/work status:** branch `feature/mirrored-artwork`, HEAD/origin `9822865e6ef82b99754e7eeba7f8dded5653fe71`; documentation changes remain local; user-owned untracked `files/` is untouched. No source change/build/test/hardware operation for this finding yet.
- **Next:** implement the small IRQ call-chain relocation, target-build and check ELF placement/diff, then return to the unresolved S98 revision mapping and F-022 status/error propagation. No physical test is planned.

### F-022 S71 IRQ call-chain relocation implemented and build-verified (2026-10-16 01:43 UTC)

- **Modification:** in `source/ezkernelnew.c`, marked `UIAudio_Timer1IRQ`, `UIAudio_StopFromTimer`, and `UIAudio_StopHardware` with `IWRAM_CODE`. The compiler inlined the intermediate `UIAudio_StopFromTimer`; both emitted functions are in IWRAM. This lets the active audio timer interrupt stop DMA/timers without fetching instructions from S71 while its metadata sector is erasing/programming.
- **Validation:** `make` succeeded and produced the target `.gba`/`.elf`. `arm-none-eabi-objdump -h` shows `.iwram` size `0x3c30`; `objdump -t` locates `UIAudio_StopHardware` at `0x030011a0`, size `0x54` (84 bytes), and `UIAudio_Timer1IRQ` at `0x030011f4`, size `0x0c` (12 bytes), both in `.iwram`; `IntrMain` is also in `.iwram` at `0x030039e4`. Disassembly confirms Timer1 handler calls only `UIAudio_StopHardware` in IWRAM and that the helper's literal references are MMIO or IWRAM audio state, with no `.text` calls. Static IWRAM/data ends at `0x03007d38`; IRQ stack starts at `__sp_irq=0x03007fa0`, leaving 616 bytes between them. Main/user stack is in EWRAM at `0x0203f000`. `git diff --check` passes.
- **Outcome/limits:** the source-level unsafe flash fetch is removed for the registered Timer1 ISR on this build; VBlank uses only the IWRAM dispatcher/default path. Audio completion remains interrupt-driven. No physical cartridge test was run; the hardware-specific crash prevention is build-verified, not experimentally reproduced.
- **Repository state:** tracked changes are limited to the two audit documents and `source/ezkernelnew.c`; ignored build outputs were regenerated. User-owned `files/` remains untracked and untouched. No commit or push yet.
- **Next:** inspect final diff and ensure the code change is narrowly scoped; commit/push this validated correction and checkpoint if no unrelated changes appear. Then continue F-022 chip/revision identification and status/error propagation.

### F-022 VBlank wait and linked execution placement follow-up (2026-10-16 01:28 UTC)

- **Phase/subsystem:** NOR liveness / interrupt behavior. **Objective:** resolve whether `Chip_Erase`'s `REG_IME=0` followed by `VBlankIntrWait()` is a guaranteed deadlock and inspect code placement during NOR busy time.
- **Investigation:** verified `main()` calls `irqInit()`, `irqEnable(IRQ_VBLANK)`, and sets `REG_IME=1` (`source/ezkernelnew.c:14461-14466`). libgba source shows `irqEnable` configures DISPSTAT, IE, and IME, while its dispatcher mirrors enabled IRQ flags into the BIOS `IntrWait` word. The hardware-checked GBA BIOS reference says `IntrWait` force-enables IME; `VBlankIntrWait` waits on VBlank. References: [libgba interrupt setup](https://github.com/devkitPro/libgba/blob/master/src/interrupt.c), [libgba dispatcher](https://github.com/devkitPro/libgba/blob/master/src/InterruptDispatcher.s), [IntrWait reference](https://retrointernals.github.io/gba-bios-reference/swi_04_intrwait/), [VBlankIntrWait reference](https://retrointernals.github.io/gba-bios-reference/swi_05_vblankintrwait/).
- **Verified correction to prior hypothesis:** IME being cleared before SWI 0x05 does not by itself cause an indefinite wait on this configured main path; the BIOS wait path enables IRQ delivery, and the VBlank source and libgba dispatcher are set up. The old 2026-10-15 entry correctly marked this uncertain; this follow-up now resolves it as **not a demonstrated defect**. The `REG_IME=1` at `Chip_Erase` return is not shown to corrupt state on its only current caller, which enters from the initialized main UI with IME enabled.
- **Artifact and address-map inspection:** `Makefile` links a map and the existing `omega-de-ds-style-multisystem-kernel.elf` was inspected with `/opt/devkitpro/devkitARM/bin/arm-none-eabi-objdump -h` and `-t`. `.text` VMA/LMA starts at `0x08000220`; `.iwram` VMA is `0x03000000` with LMA `0x081f9a84`. `Chip_Erase` is `.text` at `0x080019ae`, `FormatNor` `.text` at `0x08001a90`, and `UIAudio_Timer1IRQ` `.text` at `0x08003560`; `Block_Erase` is marked `IWRAM_CODE`. `source/ez_define.h` maps software access windows as PSRAM `0x08800000`, S98 NOR `0x09000000`, and S71 `0x08000000`. The S98 command and linked code therefore use different logical addresses; neither linked addresses nor the [manufacturer's product documentation](https://www.ezflash.cn/product/ez-flash-omega-definitive-edition/) establishes physical decode/aliasing during a busy operation.
- **Unverified interaction:** source enables Timer1 IRQ in `UIAudio_Init`. `FormatNor()` is reached after `UIAudio_PlayAccept()`; accept audio length is 2,424 samples at 22,050 Hz (~110 ms), and `delay(500)` in `source/ezkernelnew.c:1660-1665` is a 500-iteration busy loop, not a 500 ms delay. If Timer1 is still active when the user confirms formatting, its handler may run during `Chip_Erase` because VBlankIntrWait re-enables IME. Whether fetching `.text` at `0x080...` and reading NOR at `0x090...` contend on a supported Omega DE revision is not established by this source or the reviewed manuals. This remains a focused evidence gap, **not a verified defect**.
- **Commands/validation:** source searches for IRQ setup/callers and audio handling; inspected `source/NORflash_OP.c`, `source/ezkernelnew.c`, `Makefile`, and the existing ELF section/symbol tables. No build or test was needed or run; no code or hardware changed. ELF inspection first failed due the shell lacking devkitARM in PATH; using its absolute path succeeded. A `bwrap` namespace error affected filtered symbol-table attempts; the escalated read-only filter succeeded. No test failure or hardware result is claimed.

### F-022 NOR status and caller propagation re-review (2026-10-16 01:07 UTC)

- **Objective:** resume the documented offline NOR audit after F-009; map current completion polling, documented terminal-error checks, and whether failures can reach catalog/UI callers without guessing hardware revision support.
- **Source examined:** `source/NORflash_OP.c` (`Block_Erase`, `Chip_Erase`, `FormatNor`, `WriteFlash`, `WriteFlash_with32word`, `Loadfile2NOR`); `source/Ezcard_OP.c` (`Read_S71NOR_ID`, `Read_S98NOR_ID`, `Save_info` and wrappers); call sites in `source/ezkernelnew.c`.
- **Operation/caller matrix (verified from source):**
  - Sector erase (`Block_Erase`): bounded address mapping, unlock/erase command, then an unbounded loop requiring two full-word reads equal and DQ7 set. It does not check DQ5 or return a result. Used by `Loadfile2NOR` for each block and its optional patch block; also called by the NOR delete path, whose caller cannot observe failure.
  - Chip erase (`Chip_Erase`): unbounded loop reads NOR base twice and exits on equality; it does not require erased DQ7 data or check DQ5. It also displays progress with three `VBlankIntrWait()` calls per iteration. `FormatNor` clears the in-memory catalog after the `void` call returns, regardless of the flash result. The earlier IME/SWI liveness concern remains unverified, not a finding.
  - Word program (`WriteFlash`): polls equal reads after each word, without data verification, DQ5 handling, timeout, or a result. No in-tree call site was found by `rg`; preserve it as an exposed helper but do not count it as an active path without a caller.
  - 32-byte write-buffer program (`WriteFlash_with32word`): after each 16-word buffer-load command and fixed delays, loops without a bound until two reads of the last word are equal. It does not check DQ5 or DQ1 buffer-abort status or confirm the last programmed DQ7/data value. `Loadfile2NOR` uses it for ROM and optional patch blocks.
  - S71 metadata erase/program (`Save_info`): unbounded equal-read loops for erase and each word/buffer program; ID `0x2202` selects the word path and every other ID the buffer path. The `Save_sav_info`, `Save_NOR_info`, and `Save_SET_info` wrappers all return `void`; settings, save-state index, and NOR catalog callers therefore cannot detect a failure. `Loadfile2NOR` commits its catalog entry after the `void` erase/program calls and returns success; `FormatNor` drops the RAM catalog after its `void` chip erase.
  - Preceding “erase all BBP” sequence in `Loadfile2NOR`: source issues `F0`, unlock, `C0`, `0x80`, `0x30`, then performs exactly `0x15000` reads at `FlashBase_S98 + 0x5C0000`; this is a fixed delay, not status/completion/error detection. Its purpose and safe wait bound are not established by source evidence.
- **Primary hardware evidence:** official Infineon S29WS512P, S29PL-J, and S29GL064N manuals document data polling/toggle status, DQ5 timing-limit errors, and DQ1 write-buffer abort status; their flowcharts require re-poll/error handling instead of treating arbitrary equal reads as full success. References: [S29WS512P datasheet](https://www.infineon.com/assets/row/public/documents/10/49/infineon-s29ws512p-s29ws256p-s29ws128p-512-256-128-mb-32-16-8-m-x-16-bit-1.8-v-simultaneous-read-write-flash-datasheet-en.pdf), [S29PL-J datasheet](https://www.infineon.com/assets/row/public/documents/10/49/infineon-s29pl-j-128--128--64--32-mbit-8-8-4-2m-x-16-bit-3-v-flash-with-enhanced-versatileio-datasheet-en.pdf?fileId=8ac78c8c7d0d8da4017d0ed5806154f0), [S29GL064N datasheet](https://www.infineon.com/assets/row/public/documents/10/49/infineon-s29gl064n-s29gl032n-64-mbit-32-mbit-3-v-page-mode-mirrorbit-flash-datasheet-en.pdf). Earlier entries establish that published sector erase maxima exclude internal all-zero preprogram/verify, with no separate bound found for that phase. These manuals are protocol references for candidate devices, not proof that every supported Omega DE revision contains exactly these parts.
- **Official hardware mapping search:** the [EZ-FLASH download page](https://www.ezflash.cn/download/) confirms Kernel 1.06/FW7 applies to Rev A and Rev B, and says FW7 adds support for S98 chips with a `020` suffix. The official page does not name the S71/S98 manufacturers/models or their X0E IDs; the manuals/product pages reviewed do not establish a cartridge revision BOM. This confirms a revision difference but does not prove what ID the suffix returns or whether the current `0x223D` install gate accepts it.
- **Verified finding remains Medium/open:** no flash helper has a finite timeout or propagates terminal failure. On an operation that never reaches the expected read state, the kernel can remain in the busy loop; if the device reports an error state, callers can still record/clear metadata because no result reaches them. Normal-cartridge hang likelihood was not measured. Do not conflate this with an established revision incompatibility.
- **Implementation decision:** no production change yet. The exact supported S71/S98 part and revision-to-ID map is still unknown, and no safe maximum can be inferred for the undocumented/embedded preprogram wait. A timeout chosen from the published sector erase number could expire too early. Preserve generic revision support; do not add an arbitrary timeout, hard-coded ID rejection, trace build, or hardware flash. A future safe change needs a verified supported-device matrix, a defensible whole-operation time bound (or hardware watchdog policy), status parser tests, and failure propagation that prevents catalog commit/clear on failed operations.
- **Timebase follow-up:** source search found UI audio owns timers 0 and 1; no repository reference to timers 2 or 3 was found, and libgba exposes their counter/control registers. A polled Timer2/Timer3 cascade is a plausible interrupt-independent elapsed-time source for a future bounded poll, subject to confirming no cartridge/SDK integration use and setting a device-supported whole-operation limit. It does not resolve the missing erase/preprogram maximum, nor the separate `Chip_Erase` `VBlankIntrWait()` liveness uncertainty; no timer code was changed.
- **Validation:** source/call-site searches and official vendor datasheet review only. No production source edit, build, flash test, or hardware operation occurred in this checkpoint. No new test result is claimed.
- **Repository:** local commit `feec4d8` contains the manual save-import documentation checkpoint. Branch `feature/mirrored-artwork` is one commit ahead of origin; both audit ledgers now have local F-022 updates. The three user files under `files/` remain untracked and untouched.
- **Next:** seek authoritative Omega DE revision-to-flash part/ID evidence; otherwise retain that blocker. Confirm whether Timer2/3 can be reserved safely alongside all linked SDK/runtime code, and resolve whether `VBlankIntrWait` can stall the chip-erase loop. A timer source alone is insufficient without a defensible supported-device total-operation bound. Only then draft a host-testable poll/result layer and trace all callers before production edits.

### F-009 manually transferred save behavior checked against launcher source (2026-10-16 01:05 UTC)

- **Objective:** answer whether a save file created on another system can be copied into the save folder after the LFN changes, and identify filename/format conditions from the actual loader.
- **Investigation:** reviewed `SAVER_FOLDER`, `Process_savefile`, `Get_savefilesize`, `Loadsavefile`, `SavefileWrite`, and `Save_savefile` in `source/ez_define.h` and `source/ezkernelnew.c`; checked the existing in-memory FatFs long-name regression.
- **Verified behavior:** the folder is `/SYSTEM/SAVER`. GBA launch derives `<full ROM filename stem>.sav` (the emulator path uses `.esv`). `Process_savefile` opens an existing file by that exact name and uses its file length; `Loadsavefile` reads the file bytes as-is into the save memory, requiring a complete read and capping reads at 128 KiB. It performs no conversion or header stripping. On later save, the recorded save length is used to write raw bytes back.
- **Compatibility conclusion:** a manually copied raw GBA save remains usable if it is placed at `/SYSTEM/SAVER/<ROM basename>.sav`, corresponds to the same game/save hardware, and is in a raw format accepted by the cartridge. A foreign filename (for example, emulator internal title or game-code naming) must be renamed to the full ROM basename. For long LFN ROM names, use the full current basename, not the old 99-byte display prefix. The LFN work does not alter save payload bytes or the detected save mode.
- **Verified caveat / unresolved interoperability:** existing file size is trusted rather than compared with `Get_savefilesize(saveMODE)`; only a 128-KiB upper cap is applied during load. The kernel does not detect or convert emulator-specific headers, byte-order transforms, or other wrappers. Thus “copy without issue” is conditional on matching the raw save representation and size; cross-emulator save-format compatibility has not been tested. No code change is justified solely from this source review.
- **Validation:** `sh scripts/test-fatfs-long-name-host.sh` exited 0 (`FatFs long-name identity scenarios passed.`). This exercises LFN identity/path behavior on an in-memory FatFs volume, not actual cartridge save transfer, save-type interoperability, or hardware.
- **Repository state:** branch `feature/mirrored-artwork`, HEAD and origin-tracking ref remain `1ca39dbeec264e1ea592f09480c54b2a9a0341d0`. This entry and its handoff update are local documentation changes. Preserve the three user-owned untracked files under `files/`; do not stage them.

### F-009 long-name identity and save compatibility fixes validated locally (2026-10-16 00:52 UTC)

- **Objective:** close the compatibility/memory review raised by the user, including SFN identity, legacy saves, manually transferred saves, artwork lookup paths, and maximum-length backup rotation.
- **Residual consumer review:** searched all direct `pFilename_buffer` and `pFolder` uses plus all `f_open`/`f_unlink`/`f_chdir` calls. File/folder operations use the alias identity helpers; launch resolves alias to full LFN before ROM open, save naming, recent storage, and artwork/title lookup. Favorite/recent paths use bounded canonical aliases and are re-resolved before launch/display. Deletion uses the alias. Folder navigation uses the folder alias. Display and file-size reads intentionally use their separate row fields; names clipped at the 99-byte display boundary carry an alias suffix. No remaining path operation using the clipped display prefix was found in this pass.
- **F-009 result:** the 99-byte collision case is fixed in source. The in-memory FatFs regression proves a prefix can select/delete the exact shorter sibling under the old behavior, and that alias operations select/delete the longer entry while preserving the sibling. The 512-entry record is statically asserted at 120 bytes = 61,440/65,536 bytes of PSRAM. Legacy unversioned favorite/recent components of 99 bytes or more are conservatively skipped because the old files cannot distinguish a true short name from a truncated LFN; ordinary shorter legacy paths remain supported. This may hide those boundary-length legacy entries, but does not affect direct game launching or save files.
- **Save compatibility:** `Process_savefile` receives the full resolved filename, applies the existing `.sav`/`.esv` extension convention, and looks only for that full name. Thus previously accessible ROM basenames up to 99 bytes retain the same save identity, and raw external saves work if placed in `/SAVER` under that exact basename with matching raw format/size. Long names newly supported by LFN use the full basename. Manually prepared old-prefix saves for a formerly unlaunchable long LFN are not auto-migrated; probing them could associate a different exact-prefix sibling game's save. No kernel-created valid long-LFN legacy save was evidenced.
- **Artwork/memory answer:** mirror, game-code/original ROM-header, and `CUSTOM` fallbacks write into the existing thumbnail source slot and do not retain a second full decoded image. Final linked static objects include 128 KiB `pReadCache`, 32,400-byte shared render workspace, 4,144-byte start preview cache, 2,048-byte CUSTOM manifest hashes, and the widened path/name workspaces. Mirror and ROM-header full-path local arrays are 544 and 514 bytes; CUSTOM path storage is 320 bytes. The image and path workspaces are included in the measured link totals. IWRAM retains 808 bytes; EWRAM leaves 19,392 bytes between static end and configured heap cap, with a separate 12-KiB user-stack reservation. Runtime stack/heap high-water and physical rendering remain unmeasured.
- **Backup correction:** the optional backup path buffers previously failed for save basenames ≥234 bytes. `Backup_savefile` now builds full paths in 288-byte locals. Ordinary save backups retain existing appended-digit names; 255-byte `.sav`/`.esv` components encode the generation by replacing final `v` with a digit so the FatFs component stays within 255 bytes. Primary game/save identity does not change.

### F-009 backup rotation must preserve the FatFs component limit (2026-10-16 00:47:05 UTC)

- **Objective:** repair optional save-backup paths for newly supported long LFN save basenames without changing primary game/save identity or ordinary backup naming.
- **Investigation:** `Backup_savefile` builds `/SYSTEM/BACKUP/SAVER/<save-name>` in two `MAX_path_len` (256-byte) arrays. With the 21-byte absolute prefix, its preexisting guard rejects basenames of 234 bytes and longer. `Process_savefile` now preserves names up to 255 bytes. An initial shared path builder and `MAX_path_len + 32` local buffers were added; `make`, the host FatFs fixture, bounded Cppcheck, and `git diff --check` passed for a maximum 255-byte save name in an unsuffixed 276-byte nested path.
- **Verified finding, Low severity:** that first test did not cover the production rotation suffix. The function appends a generation digit after the complete save basename, creating a 256-byte final component for a 255-byte `.sav` or `.esv` name. `FF_MAX_LFN` is 255, so FatFs cannot represent that generation filename. Expected: maximum-length save backups rotate and copy. Actual source behavior before correction: the appended path exceeds FatFs's component limit; the unchecked rename/copy path cannot create a valid generation backup. Primary save data is unaffected; this concerns optional backup copies only.
- **Implemented:** `source/launcher_path_policy.h` now has a checked backup-path builder and generation-path builder. Names shorter than 255 bytes retain `<name>0`…`<name>4`. At 255 bytes, only the generated `.sav`/`.esv` save basenames are accepted and the final `v` is replaced by the generation digit (`.sa0`…`.sa4` / `.es0`…`.es4`). `Backup_savefile` uses these helpers with two `MAX_path_len + 32` local arrays; main `/SAVER/<ROM-basename>.sav/.esv` naming is unchanged.
- **Save compatibility review:** `Process_savefile` receives the full resolved ROM LFN, copies it into a 256-byte buffer, and substitutes the final extension with `sav`/`esv`. For names that fit the old 99-byte index, this preserves the same exact save basename and extension, so prior saves remain paired. A long LFN whose GBA extension was beyond the old 99-byte index was not launchable by the historical truncated-name path; newly supported long names use the full basename. A guessed truncated-name migration fallback remains intentionally excluded because it can load another game's save when prefixes collide. Manually supplied saves for formerly unlaunchable long names that use the old clipped prefix are not auto-migrated; no valid kernel-created example was established.
- **Static memory evidence:** after the LFN path/cache changes, production link sizes are `.iwram` 15,312, `.bss` 9,028, `.data` 7,612, init/fini arrays 8 combined, `.sbss` 226,360, plus an 8-byte pad. IWRAM ends at `0x03007cd8`, 808 bytes below physical end. Static EWRAM ends at offset 226,368 and leaves 19,392 bytes before configured `__eheap_end=0x0203c000`; user stack reservation is 12,288 bytes at EWRAM top. Linked symbols include `pReadCache=128 KiB`, the shared thumbnail workspace `32,400 bytes`, start-preview cache `4,144 bytes`, and CUSTOM manifest hash arrays `2,048 bytes`. Mirror/header lookups share the existing thumbnail destination; custom fallback uses the same buffers and 320-byte path. The enlarged save-backup local buffers add 64 bytes to that function's local array allocation but no static EWRAM/IWRAM; runtime stack high-water is unmeasured.
- **Implementation and validation:** the host regression shares `LauncherBuildSaveBackupGenerationPath` with production. It confirms ordinary `.sav0`/`.esv4` names are unchanged, maximum `.sav` and `.esv` generation names use `.saN`/`.esN`, and a too-small buffer fails with cleared output. On its in-memory FAT volume, the test creates/writes/closes a maximum-length generation-0 file, renames it to generation 1, and verifies source absence and destination presence. `sh scripts/test-fatfs-long-name-host.sh` exited 0. The first incremental `make` said up-to-date, so `make -B` was used; the forced complete target build exited 0, emitted no warnings, and produced the `.gba`. Bounded Cppcheck exited 0 with only informational `[toomanyconfigs]`; `git diff --check` exited 0.
- **Final link memory:** `.iwram` 15,312 bytes, `.bss` 9,028, `.data` 7,612, init/fini arrays 8 total: 31,960/32,768 bytes through `0x03007cd8`, leaving 808 bytes. EWRAM `.sbss` 226,360 plus `.pad` 8 = 226,368 bytes; configured heap cap offset is 245,760, leaving 19,392 bytes before the cap. User stack reservation is 12,288 bytes in EWRAM. Backup path arrays add 64 local stack bytes over the previous code and no static allocation. Runtime high-water was not measured.
- **Conclusion/status:** the initial path-buffer defect and the 255-byte rotation-suffix boundary are now fixed in source and covered by the host regression; the primary `.sav`/`.esv` basename remains unchanged. This optional-backup edge is locally validated but not hardware-tested. F-009 remains open until residual file/folder display/identity call sites have a final review; no commit, push, release, or flash occurred.

### F-009 compatibility policy established; fixture/build pending (2026-10-16 00:01 UTC)

- **Objective:** finish the consumer/legacy-data design checkpoint before adding a regression fixture or editing firmware records.
- **Verified path map:** directory enumeration in `source/ezkernelnew.c` stores only the first 99 bytes in both the 512-entry PSRAM `FM_FILE_FS` list and the 96-entry EWRAM `FM_Folder_FS` list. The file prefix is then used for row identity/icon/type checks, `f_unlink`, launch selection, favorite/recent path creation, and thumbnail/custom-art lookup; the folder prefix is used in `f_chdir`. Launch flows through `SD_list_MENU` into ROM open, save basename generation (`Process_savefile`), recent persistence, and artwork lookup. The list buffer currently occupies 53,248 of its fixed 65,536-byte PSRAM window; adding a 13-byte SFN alias plus a flag should round each file record to 120 bytes (61,440 total). The folder table can carry the same alias in EWRAM; its growth and final heap margin must be checked in the target link map.
- **Persistent-data evidence:** RECENT and FAVOURITES rows have no format/version marker. Old names and folder components may each be truncated to exactly 99 bytes. The favorite cache stores at most 255 path bytes, so an old row of length 255 may also be path-truncated. There is no safe way to infer which full LFN an old 99-byte component intended. **Policy:** reject/omit old recent/favorite paths if any component is at least 99 bytes; also reject favorite rows at least 255 bytes long. Keep ambiguous favorite text intact when possible so unrelated favorite saves do not silently erase it. New path identities should use FatFs `FILINFO.altname` (falling back to `fname` when no alias is provided), with a strict no-truncation capacity check for new favorite rows. Resolve alias paths back to each full `fname` before launch, title/art lookup, and save-name generation. This retains original LFN basenames for sidecars and saves while making file/folder operations unique. Existing short legacy paths remain eligible for resolution.
- **Display rule:** preserve the stored display prefix, but append the SFN alias for names whose byte length is at least 99 (including an exact 99-byte sibling that could otherwise look identical to a clipped longer LFN). Keep DBCS boundaries intact when clipping display text. Use the alias extension for file-type/icon gating when the display prefix no longer contains the extension.
- **Fixture design:** use the historical in-memory FatFs pattern from `983dd13` (temporary FatFs config copy with `FF_USE_MKFS=1`, `ff.c` + `ffunicode.c`, and an in-memory sector driver). Create A with a 99-byte LFN P and B with an LFN beginning with P; assert that the old prefix resolves to A, enumerate B's `altname`, then assert alias open/delete targets B and leaves A. Also exercise the shared legacy-path predicate at 98/99-byte component and 254/255-byte favorite boundaries. The test has not been created or run yet.

### F-009 wrong-target consequence verified in source; severity revised (2026-10-15 23:49 UTC)

- **Correction:** the earlier ledger classified F-009 as a low-severity long-name support limit. Source review now confirms a consequential wrong-target case, so classify it **Medium (verified source-level defect; FAT fixture still needed)** while retaining the earlier history rather than erasing it.
- **Affected operation:** `SD_list_L_START` passes the selected row's truncated `pFilename_buffer[..].filename` directly to `f_unlink` (`source/ezkernelnew.c:8882-8893`). Directory scanning has already reduced every listed name to its first 99 bytes (`Launcher_StoreFileRecord` and folder enumeration). FatFs `dir_find` compares an LFN through `cmp_lfn`, including the terminator/length, and separately matches the exact SFN; it does not treat a longer LFN as the truncated prefix (`source/ff15/ff.c:2384-2450`).
- **Reproduction scenario:** place two distinct files in one directory: file A has a valid 99-byte name P, and file B has a longer LFN whose first 99 bytes are P. The browser stores the same text P for both rows. Select B and confirm delete; the kernel calls `f_unlink(P)`, which resolves A by its exact LFN. Expected: delete B. Source-predicted actual result: A is deleted, B remains. The exact scenario is enabled by FatFs's length-aware LFN lookup; no media fixture was executed in this checkpoint.
- **Impact/severity:** user-confirmed deletion can remove the wrong file, with no undo path in this function. Trigger requires a name of at least 100 bytes and a sibling whose full name equals its first 99 bytes, so this is a limited but meaningful data-loss risk rather than a broad failure. The same ambiguous key can also open the shorter sibling during launch when its extension happens to fall inside the prefix.
- **Validation:** this is code-plus-library-source evidence, not a card-side test. Next validation should create the two files in a host FatFs/memory-disk fixture and assert alias-based deletion removes B while preserving A. Existing `tests/browser-installer.mjs` does not exercise FatFs.
- **Fix remains unimplemented:** preserve SFN aliases as identity while keeping display text separate; define safe handling for legacy favorites/recent paths before routing those consumers. No firmware build, test, candidate, or physical flash occurred.

### F-009 alias-consumer audit complete; legacy path compatibility remains (2026-10-15 23:46 UTC)

Style loading uses the stored name in `/SYSTEM/KERNELS`; folder navigation uses the stored folder name for `f_chdir`. `Launcher_GetVirtualFileInfo` opens recent/favorite paths directly, while list construction keeps only 200 bytes of the basename. Append the alias to clipped row labels so same-prefix entries remain visually distinguishable. Recent/favorite persistence needs an explicit canonical-path policy; aliases keep paths bounded, but must be re-resolved for full display/launch behavior.

An old 99-byte basename can be a real short-name target or a truncated LFN prefix, and a previously stored folder component may also be truncated. Treating it as a new alias or full LFN can pick the wrong entry. Any implementation must detect ambiguous legacy components (at minimum 99-byte components with longer matching names) and refuse/skip them safely, or retain explicit compatibility behavior. This is why an alias field alone is not a complete fix.

### F-009 filename truncation confirmed; compact alias option identified (2026-10-15 23:38 UTC)

- **Objective:** reconcile the F-009 handoff against FatFs's actual filename encoding and the launcher consumers, then identify a memory-bounded path to prevent a truncated list name from resolving to a different file.
- **Files/functions examined:** `source/ez_define.h`, `source/ezkernel.h`, `source/ezkernelnew.c` (`Launcher_StoreFileRecord`, SD directory enumeration/sorting, folder navigation, list/delete/launch/favorite/thumbnail consumers, style list), `source/ff15/ffconf.h`, `source/ff15/ff.h`, and `source/ff15/ff.c` (`get_fileinfo`, `put_utf`).
- **Verified storage/encoding facts:** the 512-entry list uses `FM_FILE_FS` (100-byte name plus 32-bit size, 104 bytes) at `0x08FF0000` in a fixed `0x10000`-byte PSRAM window; it currently uses 53,248 bytes and has 12,288 bytes spare. `Launcher_StoreFileRecord` retains at most 99 name bytes. Folder enumeration separately copies 100 bytes and sets byte 99 to NUL. FatFs enables LFN with `FF_MAX_LFN=255`, code page 936, and `FF_LFN_UNICODE=0` (ANSI/OEM DBCS); `FILINFO` exposes `fname[256]` and `altname[13]`. `get_fileinfo` can emit up to two OEM bytes per UTF-16 code unit, so the configured 255-byte `FF_LFN_BUF` cannot represent every valid 255-code-unit CP936 name; when conversion overflows, the LFN output is invalidated and the SFN alias can be returned instead.
- **Verified behavior risk:** the 99-byte stored file prefix is used as the input to `f_unlink`, launch selection/path loading, favorites/recent path generation, filename classification, and thumbnail lookup. Folder navigation similarly builds `f_chdir` paths from the 99-byte folder prefix. Two different long names sharing a prefix can therefore become indistinguishable in the UI/index, and a prefix that names another real entry can direct an operation to that other entry. This is a reproducible source-level ambiguity; no card-side reproduction was run.
- **Candidate design, not implemented:** `FILINFO.altname` supplies FatFs's short 8.3 alias for LFN entries and is at most 12 bytes plus NUL. Adding a 13-byte alias to the file record would likely round its size to 120 bytes; `512 * 120 = 61,440`, still inside the 65,536-byte reservation with 4,096 bytes spare. A matching alias field can be added to the 96-entry EWRAM folder records. This avoids allocating a full long-name table, but every path/identity consumer must be reviewed: save naming, artwork names, favorites/recents, style loading, and visible disambiguation could change if aliases are used indiscriminately. Confirm alias semantics and implement only after that consumer audit.
- **Validation/status:** source inspection and targeted searches only; no firmware source change, build, test, candidate, or hardware flash. The two audit documents were already modified before this entry; `files/` remains user-owned and untouched. Live branch/HEAD are `feature/mirrored-artwork` / `7aa8b52`, tracking the same origin tip; only the audit documents are modified and `files/` is untracked.
- **User steering preserved:** the user's F-022 suggestion that generic flash handling may be intentional across cartridge revisions remains the compatibility constraint. Keep unknown revision-to-chip mappings as an evidence gap; do not reject or specialize a revision by guess.
- **Next:** complete the alias-consumer audit, especially all `f_open`/`f_unlink`/`f_chdir` uses and the `Process_savefile`/recent/favorite contracts. Then choose a narrow correctness fix or document the remaining filename-support limit with explicit wrong-target risk. No further hardware trace/flash is planned.

### F-022 source acceptance matrix across flash paths (2026-10-15 23:33 UTC)

- **Objective:** distinguish deliberate generic chip support from actual device-compatibility evidence, and trace how each NOR path handles IDs and operation results.
- **Evidence examined:** `source/Ezcard_OP.c` (`Read_S71NOR_ID`, `Read_S98NOR_ID`, `Save_info`); `source/NORflash_OP.c` (`Block_Erase`, `Chip_Erase`, `WriteFlash_with32word`, `Loadfile2NOR`, `FormatNor`); `source/ezkernelnew.c` main IRQ setup and NOR menu caller; official EZ-FLASH revision notes; existing Infineon datasheet mappings already recorded in the 21:53/22:13 F-022 entries.
- **Source acceptance matrix:**
  - **Main NOR (`S98`):** the ID reader reads autoselect X0E. `Loadfile2NOR` refuses IDs other than `0x223D`; the block erase, chip erase, and buffered program helpers themselves do not inspect an ID. Existing vendor data maps X0E `0x223D` to S29WS512P, but the official EZ-FLASH note that Rev B FW7 adds S98 chips with `020` suffixes does not state whether those return another ID. Do not infer incompatibility from the exact gate without that mapping.
  - **Auxiliary metadata NOR (`S71`):** `Read_S71NOR_ID` also reads only X0E. `Save_info` uses the word-program sequence for `0x2202` (commented PL064) and the buffer-program sequence for every other ID (commented GL064); there is no explicit whitelist. Manufacturer datasheets map `0x2202` to S29PL064J and document multiple S29GL064N ID variants, but no official cartridge BOM maps them to Rev A/B. EZ-FLASH's note about an FW5 fix for a “specific S71 chip” refers to EEPROM save-type behavior and does not establish that this is the metadata flash chip.
- **Verified failure propagation:** all low-level erase/program and metadata writer APIs return `void`. `FormatNor` clears `pNorFS` immediately after `Chip_Erase` returns, regardless of whether the erase operation actually succeeded; callers cannot distinguish success from terminal status failure. `Loadfile2NOR` calls void block-erase/program helpers, then commits the catalog and returns success. Before that, its “erase all BBP” sequence runs a fixed `0x15000`-iteration read loop, not a completion/status decision; the hardware purpose and safe timing of that sequence remain unverified.
- **Interpretation after user steering:** the behavior “all S71 IDs except `0x2202` use the GL064-style write path” is verified; classifying that as a defect is not justified until the supported ID set is known. Generic handling can be intentional and should be retained if the same command/status protocol is valid across the revisions. The independently verified void-result/catalog-consistency issue remains actionable regardless of whether the protocol is generic.
- **Validation/status:** sequential source reads and `rg -n -i ... source` completed; the source search found no additional S29/device/Revision mapping in firmware besides the code above. No build, test, source change, or physical flash. `git diff --check` passes for the documentation edits; current branch remains `feature/mirrored-artwork` at pushed HEAD `7aa8b52`, with only the two audit ledgers modified and untracked user `files/` preserved.
- **Next:** establish whether any official technical or cartridge documentation lists the accepted flash models/IDs, and whether the S98 `020` suffix shares ID `0x223D`. If not, record the mapping as an external evidence gap and avoid claiming a specific revision is unsupported. Then define a generic-status/error contract that can cover the supported set, including no-success-on-timeout and no metadata/catalog commit after an erase/program error. Keep the hardware candidate/trace count unchanged.

### F-022 interrupt-wait hypothesis and revision compatibility evidence (2026-10-15 23:30 UTC)

- **Objective:** check whether `Chip_Erase`'s `REG_IME=0` followed by `VBlankIntrWait()` can itself deadlock, and incorporate the user's requirement that flash handling may intentionally be generic across Omega DE revisions.
- **Files/functions examined:** `source/NORflash_OP.c` (`Chip_Erase`, `FormatNor`); `source/ezkernelnew.c` (`main`, NOR Format dispatch); `/opt/devkitpro/libgba/include/gba_systemcalls.h` (SWI 5 wrapper) and `gba_interrupt.h` (`REG_IME`, `REG_IE`); source callsite search for both routines; official EZ-FLASH download/version notes.
- **Verified source facts:** `main` calls `irqInit()`, `irqEnable(IRQ_VBLANK)`, then sets `REG_IME=1`. The only `Chip_Erase` caller is `FormatNor`, reached from the main NOR UI. `Chip_Erase` sets `REG_IME=0` around the chip erase command, calls `VBlankIntrWait()` three times per progress iteration, and then writes `REG_IME=1`; it does not save/restore the prior value. The libgba header maps `VBlankIntrWait()` to BIOS SWI 5 and documents that IME=0 masks all interrupts. This means the call relies on BIOS/IRQ wait behavior and the previously initialized VBlank dispatcher.
- **Hypothesis status:** the initial thought that `IME=0` necessarily makes SWI 5 hang is **not verified and should not be reported as a defect**. Official devkitPro examples say VBlank must be enabled for `VBlankIntrWait` and initialize the VBlank IRQ before calling it. A widely used reverse-engineered GBA BIOS reference says `IntrWait` forces IME on and SWI 5 delegates to it, which would make this call return while also re-enabling interrupts; however, an official Nintendo SWI reference establishing that side effect was not located in this pass. Treat this secondary evidence as a lead, not proof. No device test was run. The unconditional final `REG_IME=1` is a verified state overwrite, but all in-repository `Chip_Erase` calls are through the main UI after startup enabled IME, so a user-visible regression from losing an initially disabled state is not demonstrated.
- **Revision evidence:** the official [EZ-FLASH download page](https://www.ezflash.cn/download/) says kernel 1.06/FW7 applies to Rev A and Rev B, embeds FW5 for Rev A and FW7 for Rev B, records a firmware fix for a “specific S71 chip,” and adds support for S98 chips with a `020` suffix on Rev B. This confirms that revision and chip-family/suffix differences matter, while leaving the exact physical S71/S98 model-to-revision/ID mapping unknown. Preserve the user's design goal: generic shared flash logic may be intentional and desirable; do not call it a defect or reject a revision without a verified incompatibility. The official [DE product page](https://www.ezflash.cn/product/ez-flash-omega-definitive-edition/) describes the 512-Mbit NOR capacity but does not identify the installed chip model.
- **F-022 status:** existing source evidence still verifies unbounded busy loops, no complete documented error-status handling, `void` flash APIs, and the `FormatNor` path clearing `pNorFS` after `Chip_Erase` returns regardless of whether erase succeeded. Those are separate from whether a generic protocol is intentional. Keep the finding open pending a per-supported-device operation/status matrix and a bounded policy that does not silently drop compatibility. No production source change was made.
- **Validation:** sequential source/header reads and `rg` callsite searches were run; online review used official EZ-FLASH notes, official devkitPro libgba source/example, and Nintendo's GBA Programming Manual for interrupt-register context. One unrelated combined source-read attempt failed at the `bwrap` namespace boundary; successful sequential reads followed. No build, test, hardware flash, or source change was performed.
- **Next:** continue F-022 by inventorying precisely what the current code accepts (S98 ID `0x223D`; S71 `0x2202` vs all-other-ID branch), then match each branch against officially documented supported revision/chip variants and protocol semantics. Leave the SWI/IME question as a documented uncertainty unless an authoritative BIOS/SWI source is found. Preserve generic compatibility; only propose behavior-specific dispatch after evidence.

### EWRAM stack/heap split validated in isolated build (2026-10-15 22:48 UTC)

- **Objective:** determine whether moving the user stack to EWRAM can eliminate the verified IWRAM static-data collision while preserving startup/reset behavior and a bounded heap.
- **Isolation:** extracted committed `HEAD` into `/tmp/codex-kernel-stack-audit`; only that temporary tree was edited. The temporary `source/reset.s` changed the two actual SP assignments to linker symbol `__sp_usr`, preserving the fixed `0x03007f00` startup-flag store. The temporary Makefile enabled `-fstack-usage`.
- **Build:** forced full devkitARM build passed in the isolated tree with `--defsym,__sp_usr=0x0203F000` and `--defsym,__eheap_end=0x0203C000`; output `codex-kernel-stack-audit.gba`. No warning or link failure.
- **Verified link/startup layout:** map and `.crt0` place user SP at `0x0203f000`, heap end at `0x0203c000`, IRQ SP at `0x03007fa0`, EWRAM `.sbss`/heap start at `0x02036728`, and initialized IWRAM end at `0x03007db0`. The resulting user stack interval is 12,288 bytes; the heap can grow 22,744 bytes from `__eheap_start` to `__eheap_end`. Reserved EWRAM addresses `0x0203fbfc` and `0x0203fe00` remain above the stack top. `.crt0` loads the IWRAM IRQ stack and relocated EWRAM user stack separately. Disassembly confirms `SoftReset` retains its fixed startup-flag write at `0x03007f00` and both `SoftReset` and `HardReset` load SP from `__sp_usr` (`0x0203f000`).
- **Stack evidence:** compiler `.su` records report `main` 904 B, `Launcher_DrawStartWindow` 16 B, `Launcher_DrawStartLastThumb` 1,712 B, `Load_ThumbnailEx` 480 B, `Launcher_LoadThumbnailForPath` 664 B, `Launcher_LoadCustomThumbnailByName` 200 B, and `Launcher_LoadThumbnailByGameCode` 216 B. The nested frames total 4,192 B through game-code lookup. FatFs records add `f_open` 144 B, `follow_path` 144 B, and `mount_volume` 72 B on that route (about 4,552 B known subtotal before formatting and other helper frames), leaving roughly 7.5 KiB in the proposed stack interval for additional nested frames. This is static frame accounting, not runtime high-water evidence.
- **Heap consideration:** `FF_USE_LFN=1`, so FatFs uses static LFN buffers rather than its `ff_memalloc` dynamic mode. Newlib allocator code is linked, so the proposed 22,744-byte heap cap is retained instead of assuming no heap consumer.
- **Causal status:** original production disassembly clears SP-relative local memory at `0x03007a50–0x03007b4f`, overlapping newlib `handles` data (the isolated map places it at `0x03006d00`, 4,096 bytes). Moving SP to EWRAM removes that particular alias. This is a verified memory-layout defect and a strong U-series explanation candidate; the U922 freeze's causal connection remains unproven.
- **Review correction:** the first temporary edit also changed the fixed startup-flag address by matching the wrong `0x03007f00` occurrence. Disassembly exposed it; it was restored before the successful full rebuild. No such mistake was made in workspace source.
- **Status:** production source and user files remain unchanged; no hardware flash, release, or commit. Full isolated build and map/disassembly checks passed. The temporary build is evidence only and has not been tested on hardware.
- **Next:** apply the narrow production correction in `Makefile` (linker stack/heap symbols) and `source/reset.s` (only the two SP assignments), then run the normal build and `scripts/rebuild-and-verify-audit.sh`, inspect the resulting map/disassembly and full diff, and stage at most one consolidated hardware candidate for the user. No diagnostic markers or separate trace candidate. Continue F-022 independently.

### F-022 official timing/status follow-up (2026-10-15 22:13 UTC)

- **Objective:** resolve whether published erase maxima alone provide a safe finite timeout for the main NOR and possible S71 metadata devices.
- **Evidence reviewed:** official Infineon [S29WS-P](https://www.infineon.com/assets/row/public/documents/10/49/infineon-s29ws512p-s29ws256p-s29ws128p-512-256-128-mb-32-16-8-m-x-16-bit-1.8-v-simultaneous-read-write-flash-datasheet-en.pdf), [S29PL-J](https://www.infineon.com/dgdl/Infineon-S29PL-J_128-_128-_64-_32-MBIT_%288_8_4_2M_X_16_BIT%29_3_V_FLASH_WITH_ENHANCED_VERSATILEIO-DataSheet-v07_00-EN.pdf?fileId=8ac78c8c7d0d8da4017d0ed5806154f0), and [S29GL064N/032N](https://www.infineon.com/dgdl/Infineon-S29GL064N_S29GL032N_64_Mbit_32_Mbit_3_V_Page_Mode_MirrorBit_Flash-DataSheet-v03_00-EN.pdf?fileId=8ac78c8c7d0d8da4017d0ed556fd548b) data sheets, especially erase algorithm, status polling, and timing tables; compared against `Block_Erase`, `Chip_Erase`, `WriteFlash_with32word`, and `Save_info`.
- **Verified:** erase algorithms perform an internal all-zero preprogram/verify step automatically, without host-supplied control/timing. The S29WS-P, S29PL-J, and S29GL-N sector-erase timing tables explicitly exclude that preprogram step. Their stated maxima include S29WS-P 64-Kword sector erase 3 seconds and WS512P chip erase 616 seconds; PL064J sector erase 5 seconds and chip erase 113.6 seconds; GL064N sector erase 3.5 seconds and chip erase 128 seconds. The manuals reviewed did not provide a separate upper bound for the embedded preprogram step, so these table entries alone do not establish a complete wall-clock upper bound.
- The vendor status algorithms rely on DQ7/DQ6 (and, by operation, DQ2) to recognize completion; DQ5 must be checked and rechecked when toggling indicates a busy operation, and write-buffer flows also need abort/error handling such as DQ1 where specified. The current firmware's stable full-word comparisons are not a documented substitute for the full per-device status procedure. The main `0x223D` ID matches WS512P; the actual supported S71 part remains unknown, while source routes every non-`0x2202` ID into the GL064-style write path.
- **Design implication:** do not use the printed sector maximum as a complete software timeout, and do not invent an additional preprogram allowance. A safe bounded-wait policy still needs the exact S71 part/ID set, all supported device protocols, a chosen engineering cap/UX policy despite the unbounded datasheet preprogram component, flash reset/error cleanup semantics, and caller error propagation. No production edit or test was made.
- **Next:** identify the actual S71 device(s) supported by cartridge documentation or authoritative hardware evidence; map every status bit by operation; then design a bounded polling policy and host-testable decision logic. Keep F-022 open until this is validated; hardware fault injection remains unavailable.

### Source trace analysis — U7 game-code artwork lookup (2026-10-15 21:58 UTC)

- U714 is in `Launcher_LoadThumbnailFromRomHeaderPath` immediately before calling `Launcher_LoadThumbnailByGameCode`; U715 follows the helper return. The helper uses a 160-character local path buffer and does not validate `snprintf`'s return, but the folder path plus four validated game-code bytes fit comfortably in the buffer. The lookup has several possible blocking boundaries: `f_open`, `UIAudio_StopForSharedBufferUse`, `f_read` (or its chunk loop during list-art input capture), the transparent-pixel tagging loop, and `f_close`.
- `Launcher_ReadThumbnailBytes` performs a single full-size `f_read` when list-art input capture is off; it tags transparency only when `FR_OK` and the exact byte count are reported. If input capture is on, it reads chunks up to 2,048 bytes with input polling between calls, then applies the same exact-size condition. U8 brackets the read helper and tags without marking the earlier GBA-header read.
- No production change was justified from the U714 report alone. U8 was built to gather the missing stage evidence.

### Latest investigation — F-022 timer ownership and metadata callers (2026-10-15 21:53 UTC)

- **Objective:** establish whether a source-owned GBA timer can bound the flash polls and trace how S71 metadata failures reach callers before changing flash APIs.
- **Investigation:** searched repository C/assembly for timer registers/aliases and reviewed every `Save_info`, `Save_sav_info`, `Save_NOR_info`, and `Save_SET_info` declaration/call. `source/ezkernelnew.c` configures timers 0/1 for UI audio; no timer 2/3 register use or project-local timebase helper was found in source or assembly. This does not prove another cartridge component or firmware layer leaves timers 2/3 untouched.
- **Verified caller contract:** `Save_info` and all wrappers are `void`; the erase/program status loops have no return path. Callers cannot detect failure. `Save_sav_info` at `ezkernelnew.c:9229` writes the save-recovery marker after a save is loaded, then the launch path returns success without checking the write. `Check_save_flag` at `:9308` clears the marker after SD save/discard without checking metadata completion. `Save_NOR_info` at `NORflash_OP.c:377` follows catalog mutation and `Loadfile2NOR` returns success; startup's empty-catalog reset at `ezkernelnew.c:14150` also ignores it. Settings writes from the launcher (`ezkernelnew.c:5601,9330,9448,9470`) and settings windows (`setwindow.c:1280`, `setwindow2.c:586`) ignore completion. `Launcher_PrepareSettingsFlashWrite` only waits/cuts off audio; it provides no flash result contract.
- **Verified device-ID mapping:** the official [S29PL-J data sheet](https://www.infineon.com/assets/row/public/documents/10/49/infineon-s29pl-j-128--128--64--32-mbit-8-8-4-2m-x-16-bit-3-v-flash-with-enhanced-versatileio-datasheet-en.pdf?fileId=8ac78c8c7d0d8da4017d0ed5806154f0) lists `0x2202` at autoselect word X0E and `0x2201` at X0F for PL064J. The official [S29GL064N data sheet](https://www.infineon.com/assets/row/public/documents/10/49/infineon-s29gl064n-s29gl032n-64-mbit-32-mbit-3-v-page-mode-mirrorbit-flash-datasheet-en.pdf) lists S29GL064N X0E codes `0x220C`, `0x2210`, or `0x2213` for package/model groups. `Read_S71NOR_ID` reads only X0E; `Save_info` treats every non-`0x2202` ID as “GL064,” so the exact alternate supported part/revision is not established and unknown chips are not rejected. IDs are data-sheet mappings, not observed hardware IDs.
- **Protocol/timing clarification:** the official [S29WS-P data sheet](https://www.infineon.com/assets/row/public/documents/10/49/infineon-s29ws512p-s29ws256p-s29ws128p-512-256-128-mb-32-16-8-m-x-16-bit-1.8-v-simultaneous-read-write-flash-datasheet-en.pdf) says sector erase runs an embedded all-zero preprogram/verify step without requiring the host to preprogram; its timing table nevertheless notes that the stated maximum excludes preprogramming. The kernel sends only the erase command, not an explicit preprogram pass. Do not add a separate duration blindly; resolve exactly which device operation the maximum covers before selecting a bound. PL-J's sector and chip maxima likewise require careful interpretation of its embedded erase step.
- **Status:** no production edits, test runs, or release work in this milestone. The user has no timer ownership or flash fault-injection report, so hardware timer safety and failed-chip behavior remain unverified. **Next:** finish a per-operation status-bit map for WS-P, PL-J, and the possible GL-N parts; define allowed S71 IDs or make unsupported hardware fail safely; derive bounds including any separately required preprogram/command overhead; then design pure host-testable poll-decision tests and consistent error propagation for each caller before editing production code.

### Latest investigation — F-022 flash polling and device protocol (2026-10-15 21:45 UTC)

- Re-read the active polling code and its callers in `source/NORflash_OP.c`, `source/Ezcard_OP.c`, and the NOR menu/install paths in `source/ezkernelnew.c`. Main-NOR `Block_Erase` loops at lines 85–89 and 102–106 until two full reads match and DQ7 is high; `Chip_Erase` loops at 123–137 until two reads at the base match (while also updating the UI); `WriteFlash_with32word` loops at 227–231 until the full reads match. None has a finite limit or checks DQ5; `Block_Erase`/`Chip_Erase`/`WriteFlash_with32word` return `void`. `WriteFlash` at 165–194 has a similar equality loop but no call site was found in the current repository.
- `Loadfile2NOR` accepts only `Read_S98NOR_ID() == 0x223D`, then calls the void erase/program helpers and can still save its NOR catalog metadata and return success. The vendor S29WS-P datasheet identifies `0x223D` at autoselect word X0E as WS512P. It specifies up to 616 seconds for chip erase, 3 seconds for a 64-Kword sector erase (excluding the embedded 00h pre-program step), 400 µs single-word programming, and 3 ms for a 32-word buffer program at listed worst-case conditions. Its status procedure compares DQ6 over successive reads, checks/rechecks DQ5 for timeout failure, and monitors DQ1/DQ5 for write-buffer programming. See the [Infineon S29WS512P data sheet](https://www.infineon.com/assets/row/public/documents/10/49/infineon-s29ws512p-s29ws256p-s29ws128p-512-256-128-mb-32-16-8-m-x-16-bit-1.8-v-simultaneous-read-write-flash-datasheet-en.pdf), especially autoselect IDs, write-operation status, and erase/program performance.
- S71 auxiliary metadata goes through `Save_info` at `source/Ezcard_OP.c:339-401`. Its erase loop, the `0x2202` PL064 single-word loop, and the alternate buffered-program loop all wait for consecutive full-word reads to match without a finite limit or DQ5/DQ1 handling. `Save_info`, `Save_sav_info`, `Save_NOR_info`, and `Save_SET_info` all return `void`; callers at `source/ezkernelnew.c`, `source/setwindow.c`, and `source/setwindow2.c` cannot observe failed writes. The official S29PL-J data sheet identifies PL064J device ID `0x2202` and gives maximum values of 5 seconds for sector erase (excluding pre-program), 113.6 seconds for chip erase, and 100 µs per word program under its worst-case conditions. See the [Infineon S29PL-J data sheet](https://www.infineon.com/dgdl/Infineon-S29PL-J_128-_128-_64-_32-MBIT_%288_8_4_2M_X_16_BIT%29_3_V_FLASH_WITH_ENHANCED_VERSATILEIO-DataSheet-v07_00-EN.pdf?fileId=8ac78c8c7d0d8da4017d0ed5806154f0).
- The alternate `Save_info` branch is commented `GL064`, but source accepts every ID other than `0x2202` without checking it against a specific supported GL064 device. Its exact device identity and safe timing bound are therefore unresolved. Do not infer that every non-PL064 response uses a compatible chip. The separate SD `Wait_SD_Response` routine does have a finite loop counter and is not part of this NOR finding.
- **Verified finding (medium, open):** flash-operation status polling has no time bound and misses documented failure-status checks; selected callers can commit/report success because the lower-level APIs do not return errors. A hardware that remains busy can block the UI indefinitely; a terminal error may also be mistaken for completion in loops that only wait for stable reads. This establishes the source-level liveness/error-propagation defect, not the frequency of real cartridge failures. No production code changed and no build or hardware fault test was run in this investigation.
- **Next before any production edit:** inspect available GBA timer/timebase helpers and interrupt assumptions; trace all `Save_*info` call sites and user-visible failure paths; resolve the supported alternate S71 GL064 ID/protocol; then specify a bounded status algorithm and regression strategy. Sector-erase max tables exclude embedded pre-programming, so a timeout cannot simply equal the printed erase maximum. Keep F-022 open until the bounds cover that behavior and callers propagate errors safely.

### Latest investigation — F-011 write failure path (2026-10-15 21:32 UTC)

- Inspected `Launcher_SaveUnifiedSettings` and the settings migration caller in `source/ezkernelnew.c:10931-11029`. It opens `/SYSTEM/SETTINGS.TXT` with `FA_CREATE_ALWAYS`, so FatFs truncates the existing file at open. The many `f_printf` calls' return values and `f_close` result are ignored. After any successful open, it unlinks five legacy settings files and clears `launcher_settings_migration_pending`, even if serialization or the final flush failed. This directly confirms the F-011 data-loss failure path.
- Verified the local FatFs 15 implementation: `f_printf` returns `putc_flush()`'s count or `-1` on failed/short buffered write; `f_close` calls `f_sync` and propagates its result. Those signals are available but cannot prevent the initial destructive truncation when writing the primary file in place. `f_rename` checks that the new path is unused, registers the destination, removes the old directory entry, then syncs the filesystem; replacement is not a single operation. Relevant locations: `source/ff15/ff.c:4014,4135,4216,5140-5225`.
- Startup ordering remains single-threaded: migration runs before save recovery and before the start screen; this supports no concurrent settings/thumbnail access, without excluding sequential filesystem or memory effects. The temporary diagnostic source trees mentioned in historical notes are absent from `/tmp`; only the checked-in source and ignored candidate binaries remain.
- No production source was changed and no tests/build were run in this milestone. **Next:** inspect the historical `983dd13` settings-store implementation and host harness from Git history to understand the tested protocol and why the firmware candidate was rolled back; assess whether its tests can be reused without restoring the same firmware layout. Separately prepare a diagnostic-only U6 candidate with checkpoints after the custom-loader return path, `Load_ThumbnailEx`, and `Launcher_DrawStartLastThumb`. Expected result: a reproducible phase map/hash for the user, with all production sources and releases unchanged.

### Latest action — F-011 timing isolation on hardware (2026-10-15)

- The user tested R2, which adds a 60-VBlank wait after save recovery. It booted normally, displayed `R28`, and worked through different menus and game loads. In the tested setup, that wait placement does not trigger the startup stall.
- The user tested T2, which adds the same wait immediately before the start-screen preview lookup. It failed to boot normally, showing `T20`, then `T26` and `T27` briefly, then `T29` before freezing. T2 phase 9 is a caller-side marker immediately before `Load_ThumbnailEx` or cached-favourite preview loading; it does not prove the loader's first instruction ran. This narrows the visible boundary to the call/entry interval, not the exact failing operation.
- Built T3 preserving T2's pre-preview wait. Its checkpoints are `T31` at the preview path and during the wait; `T32` at the first statement of `Load_ThumbnailEx`; `T33` after path/current-directory resolution; `T34` on entry to `Launcher_LoadThumbnailForPath`; `T35`/`T36` around mirrored-art lookup; `T37` before the custom-title lookup; `T38` at custom-loader entry before size/manifest checks; and `T39` after those checks, immediately before custom path setup and `f_open`. It is `files/candidate/ezkernelnew-timing-T3-loader-entry-trace.bin`, 2,090,904 bytes, SHA-256 `a34ffcc699271432d7d7f3b1906d53c704c4c27e3e9f542bfd62d80a3ae50763`. The hash matches the successful isolated `.gba` build, and `T3` is embedded in the image.
- The user tested T3 and reports it fails to boot normally: `T30`, then `T31` briefly, then `T37`, then a complete freeze. This means the pre-preview wait completed, `Load_ThumbnailEx` and path resolution ran, mirrored artwork missed, and execution reached the caller marker immediately before `Launcher_LoadCustomThumbnailByName`; the callee's first checkpoint `T38` was not seen. This reproduces the caller-to-callee boundary previously observed in custom-thumbnail traces. It does not prove stack overflow or identify whether the failure is in call setup, callee prologue, marker drawing, or a loader side effect.
- Built T4 to isolate that interval. It keeps T3's timing and earlier trace, but replaces `Launcher_LoadCustomThumbnailByName` in the diagnostic copy with a tiny stub that draws phase 8 and returns 0; phase 9 is drawn when the caller observes the stub's return. It skips custom manifest scanning and custom image file I/O. The candidate is `files/candidate/ezkernelnew-timing-T4-custom-loader-bypass.bin`, 2,089,960 bytes, SHA-256 `cd38f0d6c17abb0ca771ba1ed6090246da5430ecdb147120aba6c9f2c3668e0a`. The hash matches the rebuilt `.gba` output and the `T4` build ID is embedded. The build emits one expected `-Wunused-function` warning for `Launcher_ShouldTryCustomThumbnail`, which the stub bypasses.
- T4 hardware result: the user reports it boots to the start screen and loads mirrored game artwork. Custom console-folder art from `IMGS2\CUSTOM` is absent and the 16x14 system folder icon appears, as expected because T4 bypasses the custom loader. The reported markers are `T40`, `T31` briefly, then `T39`; `T31` does not match the T4 prefix and is an ambiguous visual reading, while `T40`/`T39` are consistent with stub entry and return. Booting to the start screen with mirrored game art makes mirrored lookup alone less likely as the trigger, though it does not exclude an interaction or latent side effect.
- Built T5 to test whether the custom-loader call boundary is sensitive to a stack frame. It keeps T4's behavior and adds a volatile 176-byte stack probe, touched before returning from the stub, approximating the former loader's roughly 200-byte static frame without running manifest/file I/O. Its visible ID is `U5` to distinguish it from earlier T-prefixed builds. Candidate: `files/candidate/ezkernelnew-timing-T5-stack-frame.bin`, 2,089,992 bytes, SHA-256 `a6c2a3946e88f94c4f5412bece446fab20afb44f4befe85b94ebfd7755abeb55`. The user reports U50, U51 briefly, then U59 followed by a freeze. Since U59 means the caller observed the stub return, the failure was not visibly before that point; it remains unknown whether the probe, later loader work, or subsequent startup behavior caused the freeze.
- Mirrored-art source review: `Launcher_LoadMirroredThumbnail` copies the basename into a bounded 104-byte buffer, constructs a path in a 416-byte buffer with `snprintf` length checks, and uses the normal thumbnail reader. At the title style, the maximum read is 19,254 bytes (`54 + 120*80*2`), within the 64 KiB remaining after the `pReadCache + 0x10000` destination offset; box style reads 12,854 bytes. Open files are closed after reads, and short/error reads return a miss. No direct path-buffer or destination overrun was apparent in this review. T3's `T37` is drawn after the mirror lookup returned a miss, so the lookup itself did not visibly hang; latent state/memory effects are not ruled out. The mirror implementation was introduced at `f674791`, an ancestor of both the known-good pre-F-011 parent `6bdf5588` and `92ebf72`, which makes it less likely as a standalone regression but does not exclude an interaction. T4's successful menu boot and mirrored game-art loading weigh further against mirrored lookup alone as the trigger. T5 reached U59 and then froze, so the next useful trace should add markers after the stub-return path and after the outer start-thumbnail function returns.
- The timing results show that the post-recovery wait is tolerated while delaying immediately before preview reproduces the failure, and T3 stalls at the caller marker before the custom loader, while T4 boots with the loader stubbed and mirrored art active; this narrows concern to the custom-loader call/body path or an interaction, without proving the cause. They do not prove a race with `SETTINGS.TXT` or establish a root cause. The startup path is single-threaded, and prior source-order review found settings migration completes before the later start-screen preview. Sequential filesystem/state effects or timing-sensitive behavior remain possible. No production source or release was changed; HEAD is `2520b9fb4581d7a3db01471662a30ea72d62651c`.
