### Latest action — NOR deletion menu safety and hardware validation (2026-10-15)

- The user reports the F-011 rollback seems to work and the NOR-format graphical glitch is now gone. The exact image/hash used for that earlier format result was not recorded. The F-024 crop correction is included in the published v7.4.3 asset; explicit hardware testing of that exact format screen was not separately reported.
- The user also reported that choosing a middle NOR title and selecting Delete appeared to freeze the system. Source review found the non-newest branch never called `Block_Erase`: it drew the notice and entered `wait_btn()`, which only observes B release although the footer says `(B) No (A) OK`. Pressing A therefore waits forever, explaining the reported apparent freeze without evidence of an erase attempt.
- Fixed by hiding Delete unless the selection is the final NOR catalog entry and its packed range, block-aligned file size, capacity, and `rompage` metadata agree. The same check runs again before `Block_Erase`. Menu labels, action dispatch, and audio deferral shift together when Delete is hidden. The notice is rendered on its own line between the options and footer; width uses the renderer's visible-glyph count (12 px per Chinese glyph, 6 px otherwise) and is centered within the 168-pixel popup. Portuguese wording was shortened to leave side margin. The fallback notice wait accepts release of either A or B, matching the displayed controls.
- `make` passes; focused Cppcheck on `source/ezkernelnew.c` and `source/lang.c` completed without diagnostics. Catalog JSON parses and the root/source copies match.
- The user tested the delete-safety build on hardware, reports no noticeable bugs, and confirms NOR title deletion behaves as intended. This validates F-025 for the scenarios tested; the specific title positions and erase cases were not detailed. ### F-011 rollback context (2026-10-14)

- F-011's settings persistence defect is **not fixed**: the original unchecked-write/truncation risk is back. Later hardware reports supersede this section's earlier incomplete menu results: R2 reaches normal operation, while T2 stalls at its pre-loader caller marker. The exact F-011 thumbnail-stall cause remains undiagnosed.
- The rollback target build passes (`make`, devkitARM GCC 16.1.0). `scripts/rebuild-and-verify-audit.sh` passed earlier with the production build, browser scenarios, JavaScript syntax, whitespace, and Cppcheck. The former journal-specific host harness was removed with the reverted implementation. Preserve the known-good image and leave the release unchanged.
- Removed the six failed/direct-read/trace binaries from `files/candidate/`. Preserved `files/candidate/ezkernelnew-no-start-thumb.bin` (the diagnostic the user reports boots) and the known-good `files/ezkernelnew.bin`.
- Staged `files/candidate/ezkernelnew-f011-rollback.bin` for hardware validation: 2,090,000 bytes, SHA-256 `5cda0eab1c9762e16bb38d60e3b494f008148d52163e0e440d22afe32a08cacf`. It is byte-identical to the successful `.gba` build. The user now reports it seems to work; the new NOR rendering fix is a separate build and still needs hardware validation.
- Later hardware reports supersede the earlier request to record basic menu results. R2 reaches normal operation; T2 stalls immediately before thumbnail loading; T3 reaches the caller marker before custom-thumbnail loading but not the callee's first marker. Continue one-change-at-a-time diagnostics around that call boundary. The original settings-write failure risk and the exact startup cause remain open.

### F-011 startup ordering and stack comparison (2026-10-14)

- The thumbnail loader is not normally called while `SETTINGS.TXT` is open for an F-011 settings transaction. Startup reads settings and, when legacy/missing keys require migration, calls `Launcher_SaveMigratedSettingsIfNeeded()` before the later start-screen draw invokes `Launcher_DrawStartLastThumb()`. The path is single-threaded; settings-store operations use local `FIL` handles and close them before returning. No concurrent settings-file/thumbnail access is indicated by this call order.
- A sequential effect is still possible: a failed settings operation could leave filesystem state damaged, or an earlier stack/memory overwrite could affect later thumbnail loading. These are hypotheses, not established causes. The no-start-thumbnail build confirms the freeze is in or triggered by the thumbnail call, while the direct-read diagnostic still hanging means the recovery-read scan alone does not explain it.
- Compared isolated `-fstack-usage` builds of F-011 parent `6bdf5588` and F-011 commit `983dd13` with the same devkitARM compiler. Thumbnail-path static frames are identical in both: `Launcher_SettingsReadValue` 736 bytes, `Launcher_LoadCustomThumbnailByName` 200, `Launcher_LoadThumbnailForPath` 664, `Launcher_DrawStartLastThumb` 1,712, `Launcher_DrawStartWindow` 16, and `main` 904. `Launcher_SaveUnifiedSettings` grows from 600 to 616 bytes; F-011's transaction adds deeper frames only during the earlier migration write. The map's BSS-to-user-stack interval changes from 7,956 bytes to 7,924 bytes (32-byte reduction). This does not show stack overflow; runtime high-water usage and FatFs/helper stack use are unknown.
- Current conclusion: concurrent settings manipulation is not supported by the normal startup ordering. A sequential filesystem or memory side effect remains possible, and added-code/layout effects remain possible. The exact F-011 stall cause is unresolved and needs controlled hardware comparison.

The remaining interval is callee prologue/entry or rendering T09; the result does not prove stack overflow. The larger T19–T36 trace remains reported as rejected before boot. No exact freeze cause is established.
- Generated stack review (2026-10-14): devkitARM GCC 16.1.0 `-fstack-usage` reports static frames of 904 bytes for `main`, 16 for `Launcher_DrawStartWindow`, 1,712 for `Launcher_DrawStartLastThumb`, 480 for `Load_ThumbnailEx`, 664 for `Launcher_LoadThumbnailForPath`, and 200 for `Launcher_LoadCustomThumbnailByName`. The call path reserves 3,976 bytes before deeper helpers. The linked map places IWRAM BSS end at `0x0300600c` and the user stack at `0x03007f00`, a nominal 7,924-byte interval before library/helper frames. This leaves stack pressure plausible but does not establish overflow; runtime high-water use is unknown. Do not claim a root cause.

### Cold-start procedure

1. Read `CODEX_KERNEL_AUDIT_HANDOFF.md`, this authoritative section, then the detailed findings and validation history below. Historical entries are not live-state instructions.
2. Independently inspect repository instructions (`rg --files -g AGENTS.md`), `pwd`, `git status --short --branch --untracked-files=all`, `git rev-parse HEAD`, branch/upstream, recent log, `.devcontainer/`, `README.md`, `Makefile`, `build.bat`, `source/`, tests, workflows, docs, and all tracked files. Do not assume the branch or permissions are unchanged.
3. Establish architecture and call/data flow before judging code. Inventory every firmware module and map startup/interrupts, UI state, SD/FatFs boundaries, settings/save/RTS persistence, NOR/SRAM/PSRAM, patch and reset assembly, artwork/render paths, localization/audio, build/assets, manager JS/tests/CI, and user docs. Follow callers and error exits.
4. Keep findings evidence-based: assign stable IDs; include severity, confidence, exact locations, trigger, consequence, data/control path, fix, and validation limits. Distinguish defects from false positives and limitations. Review positive, negative, short-I/O, overflow, malformed-input, cleanup, retry, and interrupted-operation paths. Do a separate adversarial review after edits.
5. Preserve work and hardware safety. Do not reset, clean, rebase, switch branches, or flash cartridges. The user tested the released firmware and two candidates: the release and direct-read candidate hang, while the no-start-thumbnail diagnostic loads as expected. Do not broadly rewrite upstream FatFs. Keep F-011 host fault-injection coverage passing when editing the journal. Do not add unrelated tests outside user authorization or an already-authorized audit gate.

- Open the repository in GitHub Codespaces using its checked-in `.devcontainer/devcontainer.json`. It builds `.devcontainer/Dockerfile`, based on `devkitpro/devkitarm:20260610`, with `DEVKITPRO=/opt/devkitpro`, `DEVKITARM=/opt/devkitpro/devkitARM`.

- Run `make`, not `make clean`. Linux/Codespaces produces `omega-de-ds-style-multisystem-kernel.gba`. Cartridge packaging name is `ezkernelnew.bin`; Windows `build.bat` copies the successful `.gba` output to that name. The v7.4.1 `.bin` was verified byte-identical.

It requires `CODEX_KERNEL_AUDIT_PROGRESS.md` to exist. Theme/image generation through Grit/PowerShell remains unverified in Linux.

Ad hoc tools may be absent after a new rebuild. `scripts/install-devkitpro.sh` was removed: it was unreferenced, duplicated the devcontainer image, and its package-bootstrap endpoint returned HTTP 403. Do not restore it without a new need.

F-009 (99-character list field vs 255-character FatFs names/64 KiB PSRAM layout) remains deferred pending a design that avoids ambiguous selection without exceeding the reserved memory window.

Existing release is already published; do not recreate it for docs-only work.

## Findings ledger

- `F-001` — Severity: low; category: maintenance/test correctness. `tests/browser-installer.mjs:184,187,203-204`. Existing tests asserted installed bundled style filenames at `v7.3`, but the authoritative `docs/manager/packages/current/manifest.json` and package binaries use version `7.4`; baseline failed on the first stale expectation. Updated all four expected filenames to `v7.4`. `node tests/browser-installer.mjs` now passes. Root cause is stale regression expectations, not installer behavior.
- `F-002` — Severity: low; category: portability. **Fixed in current worktree.** `source/ez_define.h` now declares `PSRAMBase_S98` as `unsigned char *`, making address offsets explicit byte arithmetic in standard C. The two calls to APIs taking `u32 *` cast at those boundaries. `make` passes; focused Cppcheck is being checked before finalizing.
- `F-003` — Severity: high; category: confirmed storage correctness/resource cleanup. `source/Ezcard_OP.c:88-124,127-154`, `source/ff15/diskio.c:89-113,139-162`. `Read_SD_sectors` retried a timed-out sector read twice, then unconditionally DMA-copied the last SRAM contents and returned 0. `disk_read` passed that return through as `DRESULT`, so FatFs received `RES_OK` after hardware timeout and could consume corrupted/stale sectors. `Write_SD_sectors` returned 1 on timeout without calling `SD_Disable()`, leaving the device interface enabled. The return convention is demonstrated by success returning 0 and write timeout returning 1; `DRESULT` uses `RES_OK=0`, `RES_ERROR=1`. Fixed: failed reads now disable SD and return before DMA; failed writes disable SD before returning error. Static/source review passes; no cartridge hardware access, so real timeout behavior and device recovery remain unverified.
- `F-004` — Severity: medium; category: confirmed memory safety / deep navigation. `source/ezkernelnew.c:513-516,14190-14200,15376-15400`. The per-directory selection stacks were fixed at 100 entries, while `currentpath` is 256 bytes and folder entry/restore increments and indexes `folder_select` without a depth guard. A valid path deeper than 99 nested folders can index beyond the arrays. Fixed by sizing both stacks to `MAX_path_len`; adversarial review also found the folder-entry path builder silently truncated long paths before changing directory. It now rejects a path that cannot fit in `currentpath` and updates `currentpath` only after `f_chdir` succeeds, preserving the path/depth invariant. Static Cppcheck and source review pass; filesystem integration remains untested.
- `F-005` — Severity: medium; category: confirmed memory safety. `source/showcht.c:994-1017`; caller `source/ezkernelnew.c:15790-15804,15909-15912`. `Check_cheat_file` copied exactly 100 bytes from the selected filename into a 100-byte local array and called `strlen` without forcing a terminator. The Recent Played caller can pass a 100-character basename, whose extension remains at the buffer edge and causes this code to run. Fixed by using a 200-byte local buffer matching `current_filename`, bounded copy with explicit termination, and a length check before rewriting the extension. Validation: Cppcheck/static review and edge-case call-path analysis; cartridge-side cheat loading is not run.
- `F-006` — Severity: low; category: localization catalog synchronization. **Fixed in current worktree.** v7.3 history updates `source/launcher_text_languages.json` and leaves the root duplicate untouched; the source file also contains the newer settings/boot labels and locale translations. The root `launcher_text_languages.json` is now synchronized byte-for-byte from the source copy. Parsed parity passes for all 11 locales. Preserve parity on future catalog edits.
- `F-007` — Severity: medium; category: confirmed hardware-memory correctness. `source/GBApatch.c:41-88`, called by ROM patch routines at lines 95-1441. `Write` selected the PSRAM page for the starting address once, then wrote all halfwords linearly. The same firmware maps PSRAM in 8 MiB banks (page selector increments by `0x1000` in `Loadfile2PSRAM`), so any patch payload crossing a bank boundary could write its tail into the wrong physical page. Fixed by splitting the PSRAM write into per-bank chunks and selecting each page; unaligned PSRAM patch offsets now return without issuing halfword writes. The damaged TODO comment also obscured this invariant and was replaced. Cppcheck/source verification only; no hardware test or target build yet.
- `F-008` — Severity: medium; category: confirmed malformed-input bounds/resource lifecycle. `source/showcht.c:1039-1080`. `Check_cheat_file` reads a binary index as pairs of 32-bit values, but looped while `i < filesize / 4` and then accessed `tempbuff[i + 1]`. A file containing an odd number of 32-bit words could therefore compare a word beyond the bytes read; a no-match scan also returned without closing `GameID2cht.bin`. Fixed by checking `f_read`, iterating only while a complete pair exists in the actual returned byte count, ignoring an incomplete trailing record, and closing the file after no-match/error. Existing match path already closes before returning. Cppcheck and source review pass; no malformed-file runtime harness or cartridge test.
- `F-009` — **Resolved in the pushed LFN/artwork work; historical ledger text superseded.** The full-name identity/path changes prevent the verified prefix-collision wrong-target case and preserve long-name save/art lookup. The newer handoff records `sh scripts/test-fatfs-long-name-host.sh`, target `make -B`, and `git diff --check` as passing, with the artwork mirror regression also covered. No physical cartridge/UI test was performed. See the later F-009 completion entries and current handoff; retain the 2026-10-15 discovery and reproduction history as historical evidence, not open status.
- `F-010` — Severity: medium; category: confirmed uninitialized-state defect. `source/ezkernelnew.c:13994,14121-14271` (line numbers refresh after edits). Cppcheck on the now-available devkitPro Codespace reported `game_folder_total` possibly uninitialized when `f_opendir` failed, since the aggregate was only assigned in the successful-open branch, and later used for selection clamping and launch decisions. A first branch-level initialization did not silence the warning because the main loop can transition to SD listing through additional control flow; the local is now initialized at declaration and the SD recount branch also resets it before listing. This guarantees a defined empty-list fallback. Production build passes; fresh targeted Cppcheck exits 0 with no diagnostics after the unconditional initialization. Browser suite and whitespace/JS checks passed on the immediately preceding full-script run; they should be rerun for final quality gate.
- `F-011` — Severity: medium; category: confirmed settings persistence failure-handling defect with data-loss risk; **unresolved and the journal fix has been reverted**. `Launcher_SaveUnifiedSettings` again opens `SETTINGS.TXT` with `FA_CREATE_ALWAYS`, does not check individual serialization or close results, and removes legacy files after the open succeeds. The checksummed temp/backup implementation and its host harness were removed after the v7.4.2 firmware and direct-read candidate both hung on hardware; the direct-read candidate's background/title bar/time appeared once, but time did not advance and last-played art, menu options, and controls were absent. The no-start-thumbnail diagnostic loaded normally, and thumbnail traces narrowed the stall to the custom-thumbnail call path, but no exact failing operation was established. The known-good baseline hash is `9db6fa539c7e812e653bb4b4f9fbd60a48f0c938a63849b81a83e7c364944be7`; the v7.4.2 F-011 image hash was `29c481657dff51cf6fddaae3a72654eab2565826f4e65a03c1d8998711332715`, and the direct-read candidate hash was `9da98a66aaaaf47d585abe3e44aa89d8a2b5f4dffa169b7d705608a81f78a18e`. The rollback build passed and is staged as `files/candidate/ezkernelnew-f011-rollback.bin` (SHA-256 `5cda0eab1c9762e16bb38d60e3b494f008148d52163e0e440d22afe32a08cacf`); the user now reports it seems to work while testing NOR. This is encouraging but does not fully qualify startup/thumbnail behavior, and the settings data-loss risk remains. T4 now boots to the start screen with mirrored game artwork when the custom loader is stubbed; missing `IMGS2\\CUSTOM` art is expected. The reported `T39` means the stub returned; `T31` is an ambiguous reading. T5 (`U5`, SHA-256 `a6c2a3946e88f94c4f5412bece446fab20afb44f4befe85b94ebfd7755abeb55`) is staged and awaits hardware testing. The exact F-011 stall cause remains unresolved.
- `F-012` — Severity: high; category: confirmed malformed-input memory safety in cheat parsing. `source/showcht.c:252-535,606-660,795-900`. CHEAT files are read from SD and determine section/key/value text. The parser could overflow 50-byte section buffers, leave a maximum-length key unterminated before `strcmp`, append multiline values past the 6000-byte global buffer, copy unchecked address/value digits into 8/3-byte buffers, underflow a section index when a selected record had no earlier section, and decrement an empty value length below zero before returning it as unsigned. Fixed by bounding and terminating section/key copies, resetting section lengths at each header, checking multiline capacity, providing space for an address terminator, rejecting an overlong code row without retaining partial entries, bounding value digits, guarding section lookup, and rejecting empty values before code parsing. Target build and focused Cppcheck pass; physical cheat-file loading is not hardware-tested.
- `F-013` — Severity: high; category: confirmed save integrity/error propagation. `source/ezkernelnew.c:4663-4702,9118-9199,15555-15596`. `Loadsavefile` ignored `f_read` status/byte counts and copied the EWRAM read buffer into cartridge SRAM regardless, then reported success; a short or failed SD read could install stale or partial save data. Fixed by reading the capped save into the full 128 KiB EWRAM staging buffer, requiring an exact successful read before switching/writing SRAM banks, returning failure without touching SRAM on a read error, and propagating the error so normal/NOR/FRAM load callers do not report success or continue launching. Production build and focused Cppcheck pass; physical SD/SRAM failure behavior is untested.
- `F-014` — Severity: high; category: confirmed save recovery persistence failure handling. `source/ezkernelnew.c:4702-4840,9215-9280,15630-15650`. `Save_savefile` and `SavefileWrite` ignored `f_write` results and close status, then returned success after short/error writes. `Check_save_flag` cleared the pending FRAM recovery marker regardless of whether its save reached the SD card, which could discard the only recovery prompt after a failed write; FRAM save also displayed success unconditionally. Fixed by checking every write result/byte count and `f_close`, restoring the SRAM bank mapping, propagating failures, retaining the pending marker unless the save succeeds or the user explicitly chooses B to discard, and reporting FRAM save failure. Full production build passes; storage fault behavior remains hardware-unverified.
- `F-015` — Severity: high; category: confirmed RTS integrity/error handling. `source/ezkernelnew.c:4780-4810`, `source/GBApatch.c:937-1000`. `LoadRTSfile` ignored read errors/short counts and wrote stale EWRAM data into SRAM while loading a fixed 0x70000-byte state; `Check_RTS` ignored the loader result and claimed newly created RTS files were full size even after a short/error write. Fixed by requiring the exact expected file size, checking each read before its page write, checking initialization writes and close, restoring the page mapping on failure, and propagating load failure so the game launch aborts. Production build passes; real SD/RTS hardware behavior remains untested.
- `F-016` — Severity: medium; category: confirmed bounded-string safety. `source/GBApatch.c:937-955`, `source/ezkernelnew.c:15584-15620`. `Check_RTS` and the FRAM save menu copied all 100 bytes of a fixed filename buffer and then called `strlen`; a full-width non-terminated filename could read beyond the object and produce invalid extension indexes. Both paths now find a terminator within the source capacity, reject missing/too-short names, and terminate the local copy before extension replacement. Production build and focused Cppcheck pass; maximum-width hardware filenames are not exercised on cartridge.
- `F-017` — Severity: medium; category: confirmed save-file recovery correctness. `source/ezkernelnew.c:4823-4900`. A failed first-time `.sav` creation could leave a truncated file that a later launch treated as an existing save. `SavefileWrite` now closes and removes the newly created file after short/error writes or failed close. The full audit script passed after this fix; no physical card fault test was run.
- `F-018` — Severity: high; category: confirmed memory overwrite in NOR metadata loading. `source/Ezcard_OP.c:419-425`. `Read_NOR_info` indexed `pNorFS` through a `u16 *` but looped `sizeof(FM_NOR_FS) * MAX_NOR` times, treating the metadata byte count as a halfword count. With 64 records of 132 bytes, each call wrote 16,896 bytes into an 8,448-byte array, overflowing by another 8,448 bytes. The current and known-good linker maps show this clobbered `SET_info_buffer` and cheat-parser EWRAM globals. Fixed the loop bound to the destination's halfword count and documented the unit. `make`, focused Cppcheck, and the full audit script pass. No cartridge run was performed.
- `F-019` — Severity: high; category: confirmed NOR install data-integrity and error-propagation failure. `source/NORflash_OP.c:215-363`; callers `source/ezkernelnew.c:16277,16321,16360,16367`. `Loadfile2NOR` treated `f_open` failure as success, read header fields and ROM blocks without checking seek/read results or returned byte counts, erased each destination ROM block before reading its source data, and programmed a full 128 KiB from a buffer whose final partial-block tail was stale. It also published `pNorFS[game_total_NOR]` before programming completed and leaked the opened file on the capacity return. Both PSRAM/NOR add-on callers also built the global `iTrimSize` from unchecked last-block probes; failed opens or short reads could leave the prior game's trim point active. Fixed by validating size/header reads, exact per-block I/O, 0xFF-padding the final block, reading each source block before erasing its destination ROM block, closing on failures, checking the filename bound, and committing/saving metadata only after every block is read/programmed and the source closes successfully. The shared trim-probe helper checks size, seek, exact read count, and close before updating `iTrimSize`; errors are shown rather than reusing stale state. `make`, focused Cppcheck, and the full audit script pass. Physical NOR/SD behavior is untested.
- `F-020` — Severity: high; category: confirmed NOR catalog bounds and malformed-metadata handling. `source/NORflash_OP.c:232-235,365-428`. Install could write `pNorFS[game_total_NOR]` without a slot guard. During catalog scan, the `count > MAX_NOR` check came after dereferencing `pNorFS[count]`, so a 65th record accessed beyond the 64-entry array. Persisted `filesize` was also trusted before advancing the mapped NOR address, allowing zero, unaligned, or oversized sizes to stall or leave the 64 MiB mapping. Fixed with a pre-index slot bound and checks for nonzero block-aligned sizes and remaining NOR capacity before advancing. `make`, focused Cppcheck, and the full audit script pass; malformed on-cartridge metadata was not injected.
- `F-021` — Severity: medium; category: confirmed interrupt-state cleanup defect. `source/NORflash_OP.c:374-375,417-431`. `GetFileListFromNor` disabled interrupts for its scan and returned directly after reaching the 64 MiB page limit without restoring `REG_IME`; a full-size NOR catalog could leave interrupts disabled. Fixed by saving the incoming interrupt-enable value and restoring it through the common scan exit. `make`, focused Cppcheck, and the full audit script pass; hardware interrupt behavior is untested.
- `F-022` — Severity: medium; category: open flash-operation liveness risk. `source/NORflash_OP.c:32-75,87-118,133-211`; `source/Ezcard_OP.c:339-403`. NOR erase/program and auxiliary S71 metadata routines poll hardware with no finite timeout, and their `void` APIs cannot report a failed operation to callers. If a device stays busy or stops responding, the kernel can remain blocked in the loop. No bound was added because a safe timeout needs the supported chip's erase/program maximums, its status-bit protocol, and consistent failure propagation; no hardware fault injection is available here. This is a source-confirmed unbounded wait with hardware-specific failure likelihood untested, not evidence that normal cartridges hang.
- `F-023` — Severity: medium; category: confirmed out-of-bounds read in NOR/PSRAM trim detection. `source/GBApatch.c:303-355`; caller `source/ezkernelnew.c:15809-15838`. `SetTrimSize` computed `(romsize - 1) % iSize - 1` in unsigned arithmetic. For a valid ROM whose size is 1 modulo the 128 KiB cache block, the result wrapped to `UINT_MAX`; the subsequent byte read and reverse scan indexed outside `pReadCache`. When the local offset was small, `bottom - 16` could also underflow and skip the intended bounded scan. Fixed by validating non-null/nonzero inputs, computing the modulo first, decrementing only when positive, and comparing `bottom` directly against `PATCH_LENGTH + 16`. `make`, focused Cppcheck, and the full audit script pass; no cartridge test was performed.
- `F-024` — Severity: low; category: confirmed rendering/source-stride defect in the NOR format progress redraw. `source/NORflash_OP.c` `Chip_Erase`; `images/blank/MENU.h` and `images/dark/MENU.h` define 168x110 menu assets; `source/draw.c` `DrawPic` treats its `w` parameter as source stride. The old code offset the image by `78*128` pixels and drew 128x13 as though the menu were 128 pixels wide, producing a skewed/garbled crop behind the progress text. Source history shows the bad call in the original imported source and no change to `Chip_Erase` in the recent NOR audit commit, so this is pre-existing; its first observed date is unknown. Fixed by copying the intended theme crop at source (20,78) with 168-pixel row stride, one row per `DrawPic` call. The user reports the glitch is gone, but the exact tested image/hash is unknown; the crop correction is included in the v7.4.3 release asset.
- `F-025` — Severity: medium; confidence: high; category: confirmed NOR menu input deadlock and unsafe delete affordance. `source/ezkernelnew.c`, `NOR_list_MENU` and `wait_btn()`; the menu always displayed Delete, but for any non-final catalog entry it skipped `Block_Erase`, drew the old notice, and waited in `wait_btn()`. That helper only exits on B release while the footer promises `(B) No (A) OK`, so pressing A leaves the UI in an indefinite loop and appears frozen. Hide Delete unless the selected entry is the final packed NOR entry and its size/range/`rompage` metadata validate; revalidate immediately before erase. Shift remaining menu labels and action dispatch consistently. The notice has a dedicated line between menu rows and footer, with renderer-aware sizing in the 168-pixel popup; its fallback wait accepts either A or B release. `make` and focused Cppcheck pass. The user tested the built image on hardware, reports no noticeable bugs, and confirms NOR title deletion behaves as intended. It was released as v7.4.3 at https://github.com/voluptuary/omega-de-ds-style-multisystem-kernel/releases/tag/v7.4.3 with sole asset `ezkernelnew.bin` (2,090,624 bytes; SHA-256 `c6bc008f8d10919f0100dc5545ec86edfd78b437524a8d81832d046f9d4cf505`); the downloaded asset matches. Specific title positions and erase cases were not recorded. All files in `files/candidate/` were deleted after verification. `Block_Erase`'s independent unbounded hardware busy wait is still tracked as F-022.
- `F-026` — Severity: high; confidence: high; category: verified user-stack/static-data overlap. `Makefile`, `source/reset.s`, production map, and `Launcher_DrawStartLastThumb`/thumbnail call path. The libgba default user SP is `0x03007f00`; the production IWRAM data ends at `0x03007db0` (after the fix `0x03007db8`), while the start-screen thumbnail path reserves about 4.2 KiB of nested C frames. A local clear in the 1,712-byte `Launcher_DrawStartLastThumb` frame writes `0x03007a50–0x03007b4f`, inside libsysbase's 4 KiB `handles` table (`0x03006cf8` in the baseline map). This can corrupt runtime handle state during the normal thumbnail path; U922's exact cause and whether this overlap triggers the reported freeze remain unproven. Fixed by linking user SP at `0x0203f000`, capping EWRAM heap end at `0x0203c000`, and making both reset paths use `__sp_usr`, while preserving the IWRAM IRQ stack and fixed reset flag. The 12 KiB stack interval and 22,744-byte heap cap are verified in the production map. Isolated and workspace builds pass; the full audit verification script exits 0, and `.crt0` plus reset disassembly confirm addresses. One non-diagnostic candidate is staged at `files/candidate/ezkernelnew-stack-relocation.bin` (2,090,632 bytes; SHA-256 `1d70f116eb3ff4e9c2ab2ee251d910ba96e6051f7f3d59a534f0828b2b9e5e2d`). Physical hardware behavior remains untested; do not call the U922 issue resolved until the user reports the candidate result.
- `F-033` — Severity: medium; confidence: verified. `source/ezkernelnew.c::Process_savefile`: the failed `f_open(..., FA_OPEN_EXISTING)` branch formerly treated all errors as a missing save and called `SavefileWrite()` with `FA_CREATE_ALWAYS`; a transient `FR_DISK_ERR`/path error could therefore replace an existing save with a new blank one. Fixed to create only for `FR_NO_FILE`; other errors return 5. `source/savefile_policy.h` isolates the decision. Host FatFs regression verifies `FR_NO_FILE`, `FR_NO_PATH`, and `FR_DISK_ERR`; the complete `Process_savefile()` is not host-integrated. User-visible error 5 is shared with other save failures.
- `F-034` — Severity: medium; confidence: verified. `source/ezkernelnew.c::SavefileWrite`: first-time `.sav` creation used `FA_CREATE_ALWAYS`, checked write/close but left any partial file discoverable on failure. It now closes, unlinks, and returns failure. `tests/savefile-host.c` uses repository FatFs over an in-memory FAT volume and injects each `disk_write` failure seen on the successful path; also tests a nearly-full volume. The current combined save suite passes 20 checks, including the F-036 overwrite characterization; baseline `84f680b` leaves partial files under full-media and injected-write failures. A persistent media failure can still prevent unlink; physical SD semantics are not tested. This supersedes the old F-017 attribution: F-017's pre-existing unlink was in `Save_savefile()`, whose `FA_CREATE_ALWAYS` may already have destroyed old data before a replacement write fails. That separate non-atomic overwrite risk remains open and is not solved by F-034.
- `F-035` — Severity: medium; confidence: verified parser-state leak. `source/showcht.c::Analyze_KEYVAL`: selected code rows reset `address_buf` but did not reset `val_buf`/`val_len`; a later address-only row reused the prior value when `str2hex()` read the buffer. Both are now cleared at row start. `tests/cheat-row-host.c` compiles the exact extracted parser functions: baseline `84f680b` fails with row two receiving the prior `0x000000FF`, current source passes. Only host parser behavior is validated; physical cheat application is not.
- `F-032` — Severity: medium; confidence: open source-verified interruption risk. Baseline `source/Ezcard_OP.c::Save_NOR_info` erases and rewrites the only persistent NOR catalog sector through `Save_info`; power loss during that sector replacement can make installed S98 ROMs unavailable from the catalog. A proposed `0x7C0000` shadow/header integration was rejected and reverted after the host selector demonstrated stale-generation selection when an active old shadow survives a failed retirement and legacy firmware erases the primary. The host test injects match counts, not MMIO; 0x7C0000 is unassigned in the published map and not guaranteed across undocumented revisions. No hardware flash test. Keep the baseline risk open pending a current-generation recovery design and revision-safe allocation.
- `F-036` — Severity: medium; confidence: verified; unresolved SD save replacement data-loss risk. `source/ezkernelnew.c::Save_savefile` opens with `FA_CREATE_ALWAYS`, which truncates an existing file in `source/ff15/ff.c::f_open`; on short/error write or close it unlinks the path and returns failure. Exact-function host reproduction on the in-memory FAT volume overwrites a pre-existing LFN save with injected next-write failure and confirms the path is absent afterward. The current save host runner characterizes this result; it does not assert the desired preservation behavior. `Check_save_flag` retains its recovery marker on failed output; manual save returns error 5. No fix: robust preservation needs staged/backup replacement and reboot recovery. Physical SD fault semantics are untested.
- `F-037` — **Informational evidence gap; prior likely-High classification superseded by the 2026-10-16 23:42 correction.** The code's accepted `0x223D`/128-KiB model is documented for WS512P. The candidate S29GL064S80FHV020 has `0x220C` and 64-KiB sectors, but the official “020 suffix” support note is in the separate Omega Definitive Edition B download section; it does not establish that this candidate is fitted to the original Omega Definitive Edition targeted by this repository. The original product is documented with 512-Mbit NOR, consistent with the source's 64-MiB logical capacity, while DE B is a separate 960-Mbit product. No exact original-DE chip BOM, marking, or ID capture is available. Do not treat incompatibility as likely or add `0x220C`; only reopen a conditional device-compatibility investigation if physical original-DE evidence identifies the part/topology. The 23:35 entry remains as historical reasoning and is corrected here without deleting it.
- `CL-01` — Severity: low, disposition: deferred maintenance. Claude identified 16 static functions marked unused on base `2520b9f`; current source still contains many unused-marked helpers, but this audit has not proved exact dead-code set against current linked objects. No removal without an object-size/behavior comparison.
- `CL-02` — Severity: low, disposition: documentation gap. Current source comments describe `DST1` marked-thumbnail metadata (`source/ezkernelnew.c:135-137`), but README/CHANGELOG/docs text searches found no user-facing description. No runtime defect demonstrated; add format guidance if the feature is intended for user-created artwork.
- `CL-03` — Severity: low, disposition: cosmetic. Claude reports a Windows `build.bat` missing-header diagnostic omits the `Grit\\` path prefix. No correctness impact or current verification of every batch branch; defer.
- `CL-04` — Severity: low, disposition: open release-label mismatch. Current branch `source/launcher_version.h` reports `v7.4.1`; existing v7.4.5 release source commit `0f5fe5a` reports `v7.4.5b`, despite release metadata being v7.4.5. The on-device banner can therefore disagree with the release label. Correct in a future qualified build; do not mutate/rebuild the published release here.

## Files changed by this audit

- `CODEX_KERNEL_AUDIT_PROGRESS.md`: durable continuation ledger required by the audit request. The user explicitly requested that both audit ledgers remain tracked on the development branch; update them with source changes, but do not include them in firmware release assets.
- `CODEX_KERNEL_AUDIT_HANDOFF.md`: user-requested standalone handoff for a fresh Codex/Codespace with no conversation history. The ledgers were originally local, then the user explicitly requested committing/pushing them so they survive Codespace deletion; current authoritative handling is stated at the top of this file.
- `tests/browser-installer.mjs`: corrected four bundled style filename assertions from `v7.3` to the manifest/package version `v7.4` so the existing installer scenarios test the current delivered files.
- `source/Ezcard_OP.c`: prevent stale sector copy and false-success status after both read attempts time out; disable the SD interface on both read and write error exits; bound `Read_NOR_info` by its halfword destination size.
- `source/NORflash_OP.c`: validate NOR catalog bounds and persisted block sizes, preserve interrupt state on all scan exits, and check exact SD reads before NOR erase/program operations; delay metadata commit until source I/O completes.
- `source/ezkernelnew.c`: validate trim-probe size/seek/read/close results before changing global trim state, and show a file error when a NOR install path fails.
- `source/GBApatch.c`: prevent unsigned underflow in the trim scan from indexing outside the shared 128 KiB buffer.
- `source/showcht.c`: ensure the selected game filename is terminated before calculating/replacing its extension in cheat-file lookup.
- `source/showcht.c`: restrict GameID index traversal to complete records returned by FatFs and close the index file after failed/no-match scans.
- `source/showcht.c`: bound malformed cheat section/key/value parsing, maintain string termination, prevent buffer/section-index overruns, and discard an overlong code row.
- `source/ezkernelnew.c`: stage and validate the complete save-file read before writing cartridge SRAM; propagate load failure to normal and FRAM callers.
- `source/ezkernelnew.c`: check save initialization and recovery writes plus close results; retain the pending recovery marker after a failed save and report FRAM save failures.
- `source/ezkernelnew.c`, `source/GBApatch.c`: require complete successful reads/writes for RTS files and stop launch when an RTS state cannot be loaded reliably.
- `source/GBApatch.c`, `source/ezkernelnew.c`: bound RTS and FRAM filename copies before replacing extensions.
- `source/ezkernelnew.c`: size saved folder selection stacks from `MAX_path_len` so nested folder depth cannot exceed their indexed storage.
- `source/ezkernelnew.c`: reject unrepresentable nested folder paths and avoid updating the logical current path before `f_chdir` succeeds; this preserves the stack-depth invariant found during adversarial review.
- `source/ezkernelnew.c`: initialize `game_folder_total` both at function entry and before each SD listing, preventing an undefined count after directory-open failure or a control-flow transition back to SD.
- `source/GBApatch.c`: split PSRAM patch writes at 8 MiB boundaries and select the corresponding hardware page per chunk.
- `CHANGELOG.md`: document the target branch's mirrored artwork and marked-thumbnail transparency changes under Unreleased.
- `README.md`: distinguish the Windows `.bin` packaging path from the Codespaces/Linux `.gba` Makefile build.
- `scripts/install-devkitpro.sh`: user-requested Codespaces installer for devkitPro pacman and `gba-dev`, with compiler and Makefile-rules checks.
- `scripts/rebuild-and-verify-audit.sh`: requests a Codespace rebuild when devkitARM is missing; after reconnect, rerunning it verifies the toolchain, builds without cleanup, and runs available browser, JS, diff, and optional Cppcheck checks. It never stages, commits, pushes, or cleans.
- `.devcontainer/Dockerfile`, `.devcontainer/devcontainer.json`: user-requested persistent devcontainer setup using the official devkitPro ARM image, avoiding the Codespace's 403-blocked package bootstrap host and exposing `DEVKITPRO`/`DEVKITARM` on container creation/rebuild. Configuration added but image build/install has not yet been run.
- No localization catalog was changed: the v7.3 history and current references indicate a probable stale duplicate, but do not establish a safe authoritative source or canonical translation merge.

## Commands and results

- `git status --short --branch`: clean `feature/mirrored-artwork`, tracking `origin/feature/mirrored-artwork`.
- `git rev-parse HEAD`: `63ee36690e2d81280ff528fb1a9471a63843a999`.
- `git log -5 --oneline --decorate --graph --all`: HEAD is latest local/remote feature commit and tagged `Multisystem`; history includes four preceding transparency-related commits.
- Initial diff summaries and untracked-file query: no output; no staged, unstaged, or untracked files existed before checkpoint creation.
- `git branch -avv`: local branch exactly tracks `origin/feature/mirrored-artwork`; `origin/HEAD` points to `origin/main`.

## 2026-10-16 00:26 UTC — F-009 memory and game/save compatibility checkpoint

- **Objective:** Address the user's concern that the LFN implementation could reduce ROM or game-save compatibility; reconcile memory limits and verify the save naming behavior before further F-009 edits.
- **Repository state:** Branch `feature/mirrored-artwork`, HEAD `78c3f4e` (`Record F-009 wrong-target deletion risk`), tracking origin. Tracked LFN edits and audit-document edits are in the worktree. New audit files: `scripts/test-fatfs-long-name-host.sh`, `source/launcher_path_policy.h`, and `tests/fatfs-long-name-host.c`. Preserve the untracked `files/` tree. `make` wrote ignored root `.elf` and `.gba` artifacts; no cleanup was run.
- **Evidence examined:** `source/ezkernel.h`, `source/ezkernelnew.c` directory record, resolver, launch, and save code; `source/ez_define.h`; `Makefile`; original `HEAD:source/ezkernelnew.c` enumeration, launchability, and `Process_savefile` paths; current ELF section sizes; official EZ-FLASH Omega DE [product specifications](https://www.ezflash.cn/product/ez-flash-omega-definitive-edition/) and [guide](https://www.ezflash.cn/omegade-en.pdf).
- **Verified memory facts:** `MAX_files == 512`, `sizeof(FM_FILE_FS) == 120`, and a compile-time bound fits the 64 KiB reserved PSRAM window: 61,440 bytes used, 4,096 bytes spare. Current target build passes. ELF `.sbss` is 225,848 bytes; the configured EWRAM heap cap at `0x0203c000` leaves about 19,904 bytes between `.sbss` end and that cap. This does not measure runtime heap or stack high-water. Official product data gives 256 Mbit (32 MiB) PSRAM and 512 Mbit (64 MiB) NOR. Current firmware explicitly rejects SD->PSRAM launch above 32 MiB; the official guide says 64 MiB commercial movie ROMs must be written to NOR. LFN index storage is reused/volatile while browsing and the selected filename is copied out before game loading. Thus the directory index does not consume the ROM's PSRAM capacity during execution, though the build alone does not prove hardware mapping behavior.
- **Verified compatibility limits:** Existing `Get_saveMODE` checks the same table/manual save mode selections, and `Get_savefilesize` lists SRAM 32 KiB, EEPROM 512 B/8 KiB, Flash 64/128 KiB, emulator 64 KiB, plus a 64 KiB unknown default. LFN changes do not add support for every extant ROM size or save chip. “Every known GBA ROM/save type” is not supported as a universal claim.
- **Likely defect found in current LFN patch:** `Process_savefile` falls back to a `.sav`/`.esv` name derived from the first 99 bytes when the new full-name save is absent. A long basename with an earlier period can produce a fallback key equal to another game's short save name. The code opens that file, loads it as the selected ROM's save, and records the fallback name for recovery, so a later recovery write can overwrite the other game's save. This is a plausible, directly traceable collision; no runtime test has yet been run. Review of original HEAD shows names were indexed to 99 bytes and the selected indexed name was used for launchability checks, so a normal `.gba` suffix beyond byte 99 could not pass the old launchability check. Files up to 99 bytes retain the same save key already. The fallback therefore lacks a demonstrated ordinary migration need; remove it to avoid cross-game save association.
- **Build result:** `make` PASS on the current LFN source. Two compiler warnings remain: unused local `FILINFO info` in `Launcher_ResolveDirectoryEntry`, and misleading indentation after the early bounds return in `Launcher_StyleGetLine`. These should be fixed before the next validation pass.
- **Open work:** Remove or otherwise make the legacy save fallback collision-safe; preserve the exact existing basename `.sav` behavior for ordinary names. Fix build warnings. Continue full LFN caller review and rerun `sh scripts/test-fatfs-long-name-host.sh`, `make`, and `git diff --check`. Do not claim hardware-level validation. Then checkpoint and resume F-022.
## 2026-10-16 00:35 UTC — F-009 thumbnail working-memory and lookup review

- **Objective:** Confirm that the LFN change and mirrored artwork lookup leave enough GBA working memory for list/carousel thumbnails, ROM-header game-code lookup, and `CUSTOM` artwork fallbacks.
- **Investigation:** Inspected linked `omega-de-ds-style-multisystem-kernel.elf` section sizes and `nm` symbol sizes; traced `Launcher_LoadThumbnailForPath`, `Launcher_LoadMirroredThumbnail`, `Launcher_LoadThumbnailFromRomHeaderPath`, `Launcher_LoadThumbnailByGameCode`, `Launcher_LoadCustomThumbnailByName`, manifest setup, thumbnail cache pointers, and `LauncherThumbnailRenderWorkspace`.
- **Verified memory evidence:** GBA EWRAM is 256 KiB; final `.sbss` is 225,848 bytes. The firmware sets `__eheap_end` to `0x0203c000`, leaving about 19,904 bytes from the linked EWRAM static-data end to that cap; the 12 KiB user stack occupies the region below `0x0203f000`. This is static/link evidence; runtime heap and stack high-water have not been instrumented. The shared `pReadCache` is 128 KiB and holds the three source thumbnail slots and list-row scratch; compile-time assertions enforce those partitions. The render workspace is a union: horizontal `2 x 60 x 40` RGB555, vertical `(48 x 32) + (84 x 56) + (48 x 32)`, or list-art `3 x 90 x 60`; maximum 32,400 bytes. The start-screen cache is `56 x 37 x 2` = 4,144 bytes. The custom-thumbnail manifest has two arrays of 256 `u32` hashes = 2,048 bytes total. The final `.sbss` figure includes these plus all LFN directory/path workspaces.
- **Verified lookup behavior:** Mirrored path, game-code index, ROM header read, flat custom lookup, and extension-default lookup all write into the caller's existing thumbnail slot. The mirrored method adds path/name work only, not another decoded image buffer. Full-LFN resolution occurs before the mirrored path and header lookup. `CUSTOM` image data also shares the same destination. The current compiled cache layout and linked static allocation fit; no OOM/fallback caused by the additional LFN workspaces is evident from the link.
- **Verified path-capacity issue and fix:** `Launcher_LoadMirroredThumbnail` and `Launcher_LoadThumbnailFromRomHeaderPath` used 416-byte local paths, short of a maximum 255-byte parent plus a maximum final component and root/suffix. Increased them to 544 and 514 bytes, respectively. This adds at most 128 bytes to the thumbnail call's local stack path and does not increase static EWRAM. The 12 KiB stack reservation remains; hardware high-water is unknown.
- **Save compatibility follow-up:** Removed the new truncated legacy-save fallback because it could associate a colliding short game's save with a long-named game. The exact legacy save path for ordinary names remains the same. Original HEAD indexed names to 99 bytes and checked the indexed name's file extension before launch; no valid migration case for a regular ROM whose extension lay beyond byte 99 was found. The supported save modes remain the existing logic, not a promise to handle every save chip.
- **Validation:** `make` passes after the save-path and thumbnail-path changes with no compiler warnings; `sh scripts/test-fatfs-long-name-host.sh` passes; `git diff --check` passes; targeted Cppcheck on `source/ezkernelnew.c` exits 0 with no diagnostics. No cartridge, ROM, or physical thumbnail behavior was tested.
- **Residual scope:** Memory fit does not prove runtime timing or hardware rendering. Save-type auto-detection coverage is still bounded to its existing 3,000-entry game-code table and manual modes. A recent-played title-display path still consumes stored alias paths without resolving them to the LFN; address this before closing F-009. Also finish the residual direct display/identity call-site review and record final working-tree state.
## 2026-10-16 00:40 UTC — IWRAM headroom correction

- **Objective:** Finish the artwork-working-memory audit across both EWRAM and IWRAM, then move LFN-sized preview path storage out of the nearly-full IWRAM region.
- **Evidence:** The final target ELF sections place `.iwram` (15,312 bytes), `.bss` (9,540), `.data` (7,612), and init/fini arrays (8 bytes) in IWRAM, ending at `0x03007ed8` and using 32,472/32,768 bytes. Only 296 bytes remain to the physical IWRAM end. `launcher_start_preview_path[512]` is a non-`EWRAM_BSS` static buffer and therefore part of this IWRAM footprint. Earlier F-026 work moved the user stack to EWRAM; current EWRAM has 19,904 bytes between `.sbss` end and `__eheap_end`.
- **Assessment:** The firmware links, but the IWRAM margin is unnecessarily tight for a buffer enlarged during LFN work. Moving this one 512-byte path cache into EWRAM is a narrow allocation change: expected EWRAM margin becomes about 19,392 bytes, while IWRAM gains 512 bytes. No thumbnail pixels or behavior change.
- **Next:** Annotate `launcher_start_preview_path` with `EWRAM_BSS`; rebuild and verify `.iwram/.bss/.data` addresses, `.sbss`, heap margin, and the 12 KiB user stack configuration. Then rerun the host LFN fixture and `git diff --check`; update this checkpoint with actual section values. A prior test assertion that `f_stat` on an alias returns the LFN was disproved; the test and recent-title code now use enumeration/`Launcher_ResolveFullPath` instead.
## 2026-10-16 00:47 UTC — Long-LFN save backup path defect

- **Objective:** Finish save compatibility review after widening save identity to the full LFN.
- **Verified finding, Medium:** `Backup_savefile` in `source/ezkernelnew.c` uses two `MAX_path_len` (256-byte) arrays for the absolute backup path `/SYSTEM/BACKUP/SAVER/<basename>`. The directory prefix through the final slash is 21 bytes. Its existing `snprintf` check returns for a 234-byte basename or longer, while `Process_savefile` now accepts save basenames through 255 bytes. With backup enabled, primary save loading still proceeds but this optional backup silently does not happen for long LFNs. This path was not found in the earlier check of primary save buffer lengths.
- **Reproduction by source bounds:** `strlen(prefix) == 21`; with basename length 234, the path length is 255 and trips `written >= sizeof(temp_filename)-1`. Maximum 255-byte basename plus prefix and backup digit/NUL needs 278 bytes, so `MAX_path_len+32` (288 bytes) is sufficient.
- **Planned fix/test:** Enlarge both `Backup_savefile` path arrays and use a small shared checked path-builder helper so host tests can cover the maximum legal basename plus backup suffix. Also create/open the resulting nested path on the host FatFs fixture. Then run host fixture, target `make`, one-config Cppcheck, and `git diff --check`.
- **Current memory state before this extra change:** final link uses `.sbss` 226,360 bytes, EWRAM heap margin about 19,392 bytes, IWRAM ends at `0x03007cd8` with 808 bytes to physical end. Backup buffer growth is local stack storage and does not add static EWRAM/IWRAM; maximum local growth is 64 bytes per two arrays (128 bytes total frame).
